## Tier 30 — Feature ideas from a second cross-project review (2026-07-21)

_Same method as Tier 28 — read another codebase's own documentation, cross-checked against `PROJECT_BRIEF.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md`/this file so nothing already-built or already-rejected got refiled. That source app leans heavily on AI-driven logging and coaching, which is out immediately under the no-AI hard constraint, so this batch is smaller than Tier 28. One further candidate (simple workout/exercise logging with an estimated calorie-burn contribution) was flagged for discussion rather than filed — it's a genuinely new domain area, not a small addition, and needs a scope conversation first. None of these are picked up yet._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#258](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/258) | ✅ Done | Add optional water/hydration tracking | New `DailyEntry.waterMl?` (additive), gated by a new `useWaterTrackingStore` (off by default, wired into Settings' unified "What to track" toggle group like cycle/digestion). `DailyEntryForm.tsx`: always-editable number field + Save (validated 0-10000ml) plus "+1 glass"/"+1 bottle" quick-add buttons that bump the total and save immediately. `Goal.dailyWaterTargetMl?` — exact sibling of #252's fat/carb targets (NumberInput, summary-table row, "Remaining water" Today StatCard, individually hideable). Included in export: JSON (no version bump) + CSV/XLSX/Markdown trailing column. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#259](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/259) | ✅ Done | Add a deterministic BMR/TDEE-based target calculator (suggest, don't auto-apply) | New `domain/stats/targetCalculator.ts`: `calculateTdee(bmr, activityLevel)` (standard activity multipliers, sedentary 1.2 through very-active 1.9) and `suggestDailyTargets(weightKg, heightCm, age, sex, activityLevel, dailyDeficitKcal)` — TDEE minus the existing weekly-pace deficit (`domain/goal`'s own `estimatedDailyCalorieDeficitKcal`, 0 if no pace typed in yet) gives the calorie target; protein/fat use fixed g/kg-bodyweight ratios (1.6/0.8), carbs fill the remainder, clamped at 0. Reused #233's existing `useProfileStore` (height/age/sex) rather than a second profile concept, per the issue's own "where these live is an implementation decision" note — added one new field, `activityLevel` (5 levels), plus a matching Settings Profile-card `ToggleGroup`. New `useLatestWeight` hook (same direct-repository pattern as `useMaxRecordedWeight`) feeds `GoalForm` the most recent logged weight via a new `latestWeightKg` prop from `GoalScreen`. "Suggest a target" button on `GoalForm` — disabled with an explanatory hint until every input exists, prefills (via RHF's `setValue`) the four existing #208/#220/#252 target fields on click, never auto-saves; captioned non-medical-advice like `calorieDeficit.ts`'s existing estimate. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#260](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/260) | ✅ Done | Preview today's new running total before saving a meal | New `MealItemEditorSheet` prop `todayTotalPreview?: string`, shown next to the existing per-item preview (#98) only while a valid draft exists. Wired up for the bottom add-a-new-meal flow only (`totalCalories(calorieEntries) + draft.amountKcal`) — deliberately not wired for editing an already-saved meal's items, where the correct number is a whole-meal delta, not a simple sum; scoped out rather than shown wrong, noted in `ARCHITECTURE.md` as a follow-up. Confirmed by the user directly on-device. **Follow-up filed as #273**: extend the same preview to the food-search picker (`FoodPickerDialog.tsx`), which doesn't have it yet |
| [#261](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/261) | ❌ Closed, not planned | Add an opt-in local push notification for the 'haven't logged today' reminder | Discussed feasibility before implementing rather than building something that couldn't deliver what it promised: a genuinely reliable local push (no server, fires while backgrounded/closed) isn't achievable on iOS Safari/PWA — iOS suspends web JS almost immediately when backgrounded, no Periodic Background Sync support, and real Web Push (iOS 16.4+) requires a server to trigger it, which this app doesn't have and isn't taking on for one feature. Desktop works fine (background timer), Android Chrome is partial-at-best (Periodic Background Sync, imprecise timing) — but since iOS is a priority platform, a "best-effort" version would only fire the moment the app reopens anyway, no real improvement over the existing in-app banner (#171). Closed without implementing; revisit if/when a native iOS app exists, since a real app can register genuine OS-level notifications without needing a backend |

Also added: a scope-note comment on already-filed #253, suggesting the copy-source day could be picker-selectable rather than hardcoded to "yesterday" — not worth a separate issue.

## Tier 31 — Found while implementing #233 (2026-07-22)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#262](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/262) | ✅ Done | Fixed bottom tab bar reappearing on text-input blur can swallow the very next tap near the bottom of the viewport | Root-caused precisely via a live event trace (Playwright, capturing every pointerdown/mousedown/focusout/focusin/pointerup/mouseup/click with its real target): a first fix attempt (deferring only the `focusout` handler) still failed, because `useIsTextInputFocused.ts`'s `focusin` listener *also* set state directly and synchronously — focusing a non-text control (e.g. the clicked `ToggleGroupItem` button itself) fired `focusin` with that button as `document.activeElement`, synchronously flipping the bar back on *before* the same click's `pointerup` had even been dispatched, so the reappearing bar intercepted `pointerup`/`mouseup`/`click`. Real fix: any transition *out* of a keyboard-opening element — whether reported via `focusout` or via `focusin` landing on a non-keyboard element — now waits for that gesture's own `pointerup` (with a 100ms fallback for a focus change with no pointer involved, e.g. Tab key) before applying; only the transition *into* a keyboard-opening element stays immediate, since hiding the bar can never itself steal a click. Confirmed fixed via the same event trace (pointerup/mouseup/click all land on the real target now) and a new `AppShell.test.tsx` regression test. Closed without a separate device-validation pause — a deterministic logic/timing fix with reproducible before/after proof, not a subjective visual judgment call |

Traced the real cause through `reloadForUpdate.ts`/`useAppUpdateAvailable.ts`: no backend exists for Redis (or any server-side cache) to sit in front of — the delay is the service-worker update pipeline itself (bounded 5s `controllerchange` wait, GitHub Pages' CDN caching `sw.js` per #211, re-downloading the ~2MB precache on a genuine update, then a full SPA reboot). User picked 2 of the 4 discussed directions via `AskUserQuestion`: (1) `reloadForUpdate()` now skips the 5s `controllerchange` wait entirely when `registration.installing`/`.waiting` are both unset after `update()` resolves; (2) `vite.config.ts`'s workbox `globIgnores` now excludes the `exceljs` chunk from the precache (~37% smaller, confirmed via a real build). On-device result: ~15s → ~11s → confirmed further down to **~6s**, which the user considers acceptable. Closed without further changes |

## Tier 33 — Barcode-scanning follow-ups and fasting-window feedback (2026-07-22)

_Live feedback right after #256/#257 shipped and were validated. Sorted easiest-to-hardest at the user's request; being worked through in that order._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#285](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/285) | ✅ Done | Imported favorites don't show in Settings food list until the add-modal reloads the store | Follow-up to #284. Rewrote the issue mid-session — original text described an in-session toggle-sync bug; the real trigger is a fresh **import** on a new device. Root-caused precisely: `exportActions.ts`'s `importAllData` writes straight to IndexedDB through its own module-scope repository instances, entirely bypassing `useMealItemStore`/`useFoodOverrideStore` — any already-mounted UI reading from those stores (e.g. `MealItemsSection`'s Settings list, already open on the same page as the Import button) keeps showing pre-import data until something unrelated happens to remount and reload them (opening `FoodPickerDialog` elsewhere just happens to, since `MealList.tsx` calls `loadItems()` on its own mount and the store is shared app-wide). Fixed at the call site, not the stores themselves — `ExportSection.tsx`'s `handleImportFile` now calls `useMealItemStore.getState().loadItems()`/`useFoodOverrideStore.getState().loadOverrides()` right after a successful `importAllData`. Fixed `useFoodOverrideStore` too, not just `useMealItemStore` — identical write-bypass shape, and #276 gave `FoodOverride` its own `favorite` field, so it was exposed to the exact same symptom class. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#287](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/287) | ✅ Done | Show a toast with the fasting-window duration when logging the day's first meal | New `fastingHoursBetween(previousDayEntry, currentDayEntry)` factored out of `fastingWindowPoints` (`domain/stats/fastingWindow.ts`) — same elapsed-hours midnight-spanning math, minus `fastingWindowPoints`' own weight-logged requirement (irrelevant here). `MealList.tsx`'s `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal(nextEntries)` fires only when no entry had a `timeEaten` before this save but one does after. Dismissible banner (`X` button), not a background/push notification — see #261, closed as infeasible for that. **First live report** root-caused to a race in `previousDayEntry` fetching, fixed by fetching fresh at save time — but **reported again as still not firing**. Second root cause found via live Playwright reproduction (seeded a previous day with a timed meal via direct IndexedDB write, then added today's first meal through each add path): `addFoodEntry()` (the "Find food" quick-commit path — the add row's own primary, most prominent button) sets its own `timeEaten` but never called `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal` at all, unlike `addMeal()`/`saveEditMeal()` — so anyone whose first meal of the day goes through "Find food" never saw the toast, regardless of the earlier race fix. `copyDaySelectedGroups`/`repeatSelectedItems` ("Copy yesterday's meals"/"Repeat yesterday's meal") were also checked but don't need the same fix — they deliberately never set `timeEaten` (only food data is cloned, not time), so the toast correctly stays silent until the user edits the copied/repeated meal to add a time, which already goes through `saveEditMeal()`. Fixed by wiring the same `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal` call into `addFoodEntry()`. New regression test in `MealList.test.tsx` covering the Find-food path specifically (every prior #287 test only exercised "+ Add item", which is why this gap wasn't caught before). Verified live via Playwright + direct IndexedDB seeding. Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#288](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/288) | ✅ Done | Barcode scanner missing from the edit-meal page | #256 wired "Scan barcode" into the bottom add-row only, a deliberate v1 scope trim per its own checklist — reported live as a gap once using the feature. New parent-owned `scanBarcodeIntoEditItems(barcode)` in `MealList.tsx` (same local-first/Open-Food-Facts flow as the add-row's own `handleBarcodeScanned`) — since there's no single "current draft" being typed into within an existing meal's edit mode, a scan always creates a brand new `EditItemDraft` (blank, local-match-prefilled, or OFF-prefilled) rather than mutating a row, then opens that draft's editor sheet, mirroring "+ Add item"'s own `onOpenEditItem(onAddEditItem())` shape. New `MealListItemProps.onScanBarcode` prop; `isBarcodeScannerOpen`/`barcodeNotFoundMessage` kept local to `MealListItem`, same "not lifted higher" precedent `isFoodPickerOpen` already established there. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#289](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/289) | ✅ Done | Barcode scanner missing from Settings food-list add form | Same gap as #288, different entry point (`MealItemsSection.tsx`'s own "Add custom food" form). `AddMealItemForm` gained an icon-only "Scan barcode" button + lazily-mounted `BarcodeScannerDialog`, reusing #256's `lookupBarcode`/local-then-Open-Food-Facts flow verbatim — `BarcodeScannerDialog`/`lookupBarcode`/`BarcodeLookupResult` added to `daily-log`'s barrel export (`index.ts`) for cross-feature reuse, same precedent `EmotionPicker` already established for #172. A scanned barcode now also gets stored on the new `MealItem` (`touch`'s existing 4th argument). Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#290](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/290) | ✅ Done | Redesign Settings "Add food" to a dedicated form instead of scroll-to-bottom inline reveal | Reported live — clicking "Add food" on a device with many saved foods revealed the inline form below the entire existing list, reading as if nothing happened until scrolled all the way down. Shape decision resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: a full-screen dialog (same `size="fullscreen"` `Dialog` pattern as `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx`/`BarcodeScannerDialog.tsx`), not a dedicated route — reachable instantly regardless of scroll position, no new routing needed. `AddMealItemForm`'s existing macro-scaling/favorite/barcode-scanning logic (#289) is unchanged, only its JSX shell moved from an inline `<li>` to a `Dialog`/`DialogContent`, restyled to the same `h-12`/grid-of-fields layout `MealItemEditorSheet` already established rather than the old cramped `h-7`/`h-8` inline-row sizing. New `settings.addMealItemDialogTitle`/`closeAddMealItemDialogLabel` i18n keys. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#291](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/291) | ✅ Done | Barcode scanning fails on a different iPhone — camera closes immediately, no match | Not reproducible on the reporter's own device, so the camera-specific root cause (if any) is still unconfirmed — this ships the two requested mitigations rather than a root-cause fix. **Manual entry** (scope resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: lives inside the same `BarcodeScannerDialog`, not a separate button): a text input + Search button, always visible alongside the camera (not just after a camera error) — same `onScanned` path either way, so typing the number in isolates whether a future report is camera/decoding-specific or a `lookupBarcode` problem. **Debug detail**: the camera-open `catch` block previously swallowed the real error entirely; `scanBarcodeCameraErrorMessage` is now a function that appends the caught error's `.name` (e.g. "NotAllowedError") in parens to the existing on-screen message — already user-visible copy a report can quote verbatim, no new logging infrastructure needed. Also benefits from #292 (see below) — a slow/failing lookup with no loading state could easily have read as "camera closed immediately, nothing happened" on its own. Confirmed by the user directly on-device — can scan barcodes now |
| [#292](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/292) | ✅ Done | Barcode scan has no loading indicator between decode and lookup finishing | Root cause was `BarcodeScannerDialog` calling `onOpenChange(false)` the instant a barcode decoded, without waiting for the caller's async `onScanned` (the `lookupBarcode` call) to finish — the gap between that close and the item sheet/no-match message appearing had zero visible feedback. Fixed by making the dialog `await` `onScanned` (now typed `(barcode) => void \| Promise<void>`) before closing, showing a "Searching for this product…" message for that whole gap instead of closing early; the camera decode loop is also stopped immediately on a successful decode (`controlsRef.current?.stop()`) so a stray second callback can't fire mid-lookup. Any unexpected throw from `onScanned` is now caught and logged (`console.error`) rather than becoming an unhandled rejection. Confirmed by the user directly on-device. **Follow-up filed as #294**: the indicator is only visible briefly — most of the scan time is actually spent in the camera-decode phase itself (before a barcode is even found), which has no feedback of its own |

Also filed and resolved same-session: [#286](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/286) (fasting eating-cutoff removal, see Tier 29 above).

## Tier 34 — Live feedback after recipes/barcode follow-ups shipped (2026-07-23)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#293](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/293) | ✅ Done (not reproducible) | Scanned barcode food doesn't get added to the saved-foods list | Live-tested all three scan entry points via Playwright (mocked Open Food Facts fetch, manual-entry barcode input from #291) with the resulting `MealItem` read straight out of IndexedDB rather than trusted from the UI — all three correctly persisted name/macros/barcode and rendered in Settings' list after a full reload. Could not reproduce; left open rather than assumed-fixed per this repo's own precedent that a clean desktop-Chromium run doesn't disprove a live device report, and asked for more repro detail if it recurred. Confirmed directly by the user that it no longer reproduces — closed with no code change (nothing was ever actually broken in the paths tested) |
| [#294](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/294) | ✅ Done | Barcode camera-decode phase feels slow, with no feedback of its own | Picked 3 of the 4 untried directions the issue listed, skipping the lower-capture-resolution one since its own stated tradeoff (needing the barcode held closer/steadier) wasn't a clear win. **Format restriction**: `BrowserMultiFormatReader` now constructed with a `DecodeHintType.POSSIBLE_FORMATS` hint restricting it to UPC-A/UPC-E/EAN-13/EAN-8 (Open Food Facts' actual formats) instead of all ~18 symbologies it checks by default — less work per frame, a real speed improvement. **Scan-target overlay**: a framing-box guide (`aspect-[5/2]`, matching a barcode's own wide-short shape) over the live camera feed, purely visual — decoding itself still scans the full frame. **"Still scanning" tip**: appears after 4 seconds if nothing's decoded yet, reusing the exact wording the issue itself suggested. New i18n key `scanBarcodeStillScanningTip` (en/ru). New tests in `BarcodeScannerDialog.test.tsx` — the format-restriction one captures the constructor's hints via the existing mock class rather than replicating zxing's real hint-processing; the tip test uses a `setTimeout` spy rather than fake timers, which proved unreliable alongside this component's real dynamic-import/Promise chain and React's own scheduler. Live-verified via Playwright with Chromium's fake camera device (`--use-fake-device-for-media-stream`) — overlay renders correctly, tip appears in the DOM after the delay (confirmed via query since the dialog's own internal scroll clipped it from a full-page screenshot). Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#295](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/295) | ✅ Done | Log recipe button caused the add-row button cluster to overflow horizontally | Reported live with a Russian-locale screenshot right after #251 shipped. Root cause: `MealList.tsx`'s add-row (`+ Add item`, its clear button, `Scan barcode`, now `Log recipe`) packed all 4 triggers into one non-wrapping `flex items-center gap-2` row — a 4th trigger, especially with longer Russian labels ("Сканировать штрихкод", "Записать рецепт"), pushed the row past the viewport width. Fixed with `flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2` + `min-w-0` on the `+ Add item` button (needed for its `truncate`-able inner span to actually shrink). Verified via a real headless-browser check (390×844 viewport, RU locale): `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 390`, no horizontal overflow, screenshot confirms the row wraps onto two lines. Given the `layout-breaking` label. Full suite green (1166 tests). Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#296](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/296) | ✅ Done | No way to set a meal's time when adding via Find food or + Add item | Root-caused precisely: "+ Add item" (`addMeal()`) already reads the add-row's own `addTime` field correctly — only "Find food" (`addFoodEntry()`) was the actual gap, unconditionally stamping `currentTimeHHMM()` regardless of whether the user had already set a time in that same visible field. Fixed to use `addTime` when set, falling back to the current time otherwise (unchanged default for the common case), and clears `addTime` after committing, same cleanup `addMeal()` already does. `addFoodToEditItems` (Find food inside an existing meal's edit) needed no change — it never touched `timeEaten`, the meal's own already-settable `editGroupTime` handles that case. Deliberately no new time inputs added inside the Add food/Find food modals themselves — the user confirmed the one existing meal-level Time field is enough. New test in `DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`; verified live via Playwright. Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#297](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/297) | ✅ Done | Allow drag-and-drop reordering of Dashboard sections | Both open questions resolved the way the issue itself expected: `@dnd-kit` for drag mechanics (already used for meal reordering in `MealList.tsx`, same `DndContext`/`SortableContext`/`arrayMove` pattern reused verbatim), and a new `dashboardSectionOrderStore.ts` (localStorage-persisted) for order — reuses the existing `DashboardChartKey` type from `dashboardChartVisibilityStore.ts` rather than a second parallel key list, since it's the same 17 sections either store needs to know about. A drag-handle strip (grip icon) added above each section at the `DashboardScreen.tsx` level, not inside any individual chart/card component — touching 15+ files each with their own already-established title layout (#232/#245/#247) would have been much more invasive than one small additive wrapper. Persisted order reconciles against the current section list on load (drops removed keys, appends new ones at the end) so a future new Dashboard section doesn't silently fail to render just because it's absent from an old save. New tests in `DashboardScreen.test.tsx`, using the same `capturedOnDragEnd` mock-and-invoke-directly pattern `DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`'s own meal-reorder tests already established (jsdom can't produce real drag gestures). Live-verified via Playwright: dragged "Weight trend"'s handle down past "Calorie trend", order swapped on screen and survived a reload. Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#298](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/298) | ✅ Done | Let the user configure a custom day-start time instead of midnight | Design fork resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before starting, exactly as flagged when filed: (1) time between midnight and the configured start still counts as the *previous* day (not already the new day), (2) first-pass scope is the Today screen only — streaks, weekly/monthly summaries, correlation day-pairing, and the fasting-window toast are explicitly deferred to a follow-up rather than reworked all at once, (3) a Settings toggle, forward-only (no retroactive re-bucketing of already-logged history). New `domain/stats/dayStart.ts`'s `effectiveDateFor(now, dayStartTime)` — before the configured time, resolves to the previous calendar day; at/after it, the real calendar day. New `useDayStartStore` (localStorage, `'HH:MM'`, default `'00:00'` — exactly today's existing behavior, so this is a no-op for anyone who never touches the setting). `TodayScreen.tsx`'s own `todayIso()` now reads through this instead of a bare `format(new Date(), ...)` — used for the default landing date, the date-picker's `max`, the next-day arrow's disabled state, the goal-renewal-reminder comparison, and the daily-reminder gate (every "today" this screen itself computes). New Settings card (time input, mirroring the existing Week start card's shape) right after Week start. New tests in `dayStart.test.ts` and `TodayScreen.test.tsx` (fixed-clock, both sides of the boundary, plus a default-midnight-is-a-no-op case); one existing `SettingsScreen.test.tsx` keyboard-tab-order test updated for the new focusable field. Live-verified via Playwright's `page.clock` API (fixed browser time at 01:30 with a 03:00 day-start showed 07/23; advancing to 03:30 showed 07/24). Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#299](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/299) | ✅ Done | Add-row's "Log recipe" button has no icon, unlike its siblings | Added `ChefHat` (`lucide-react`) next to the button's label, same shape "Scan barcode" already uses. Live-verified via Playwright screenshot. Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |

## Tier 35 — Live feedback while working Tier 34 (2026-07-23)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#300](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/300) | ✅ Done | Closing the barcode-scan item sheet via X still adds the food to the meal | Confirmed exactly the guessed root cause: `scanBarcodeIntoEditItems` (existing-meal-edit scan path) stages a new draft into `editItems` the instant the lookup resolves, and the sheet's own X/Escape/backdrop dismissal (routed through `onOpenChange`) previously only closed the sheet, never removed that draft — so the meal's own Save still committed it. Fixed by checking, in that same `onOpenChange` handler, whether the closing draft is both new (`isOpenDraftNew`) and scan-populated (`barcode !== undefined`) — if so, `onRemoveEditItem` instead of just closing. `onSave`/`onSaveAndAddAnother` are untouched (separate click handlers, not routed through `onOpenChange`), so explicitly confirming a scan still works exactly as before; "+ Add item"'s blank draft is also unaffected (no `barcode`, so it keeps falling through to the existing close-only behavior, same as always — its blank state already got silently filtered out at save time regardless). **Reported still reproducing** (a second time, on the add-row's own scan, not edit-mode): `handleBarcodeScanned` (add-row) doesn't stage into an array, so nothing was ever actually added to the meal (0 kcal, confirmed) — but the prefilled draft stayed in the add-row's own fields, and #151/#153's "preview the current draft in place of + Add item" then rendered it as a chip reading like an already-added item. Fixed the same way: the add-item sheet's `onOpenChange` now resets the draft when closed via X and the draft came from a scan, same manually-typed-drafts-unaffected reasoning as above. **Also fixed in the same pass**: the preview chip and its clear-✕ were separate flex children of the wrapping trigger row, so a long preview (a scanned product's name, especially in Russian) could push just the ✕ onto its own second line — grouped into one `min-w-0` flex container so the wrap now treats the pair as a single unit. Live-verified via Playwright (scan → sheet prefilled → X → save the meal → scanned item absent from the log; add-row scan → X → draft cleared back to plain "+ Add item"; long Russian name → ✕ stays glued to the title). New regression tests in `MealList.test.tsx`. Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#301](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/301) | ✅ Done | Fasting-window toast stays visible after deleting the meal that triggered it | Confirmed the guessed root cause: the toast value was only ever set, never reset. Fixed in `confirmDeleteMeal()` — after the delete, if the day no longer has *any* timed meal left (covers the reported case: deleting the only meal, the one that triggered the toast), the toast is dismissed. Deliberately scoped to "no timed meal left at all" rather than tracking exactly which meal produced the shown value — simpler, and matches the reported scenario precisely; a rarer case (multiple timed meals, deleting specifically the one that was earliest) would leave a technically-stale hours figure showing, not chased down here. New regression test in `MealList.test.tsx` (needed a small `ControlledMealList` test wrapper — a real `onChange` state loop — since most existing tests' `onChange={vi.fn()}` never feeds a save back into the component's own props, which this test's delete step needed to see). Superseded by the same session's follow-up fix below (the toast moved from local state into a shared store) — this fix's behavior is unchanged, just now implemented against `dismissFastingWindowToast()` instead of a local setter. Live-verified via Playwright + IndexedDB seeding. Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#287](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/287) reopened again | ✅ Done | Fasting-window toast never showed when a time was added to an already-logged meal via its dedicated edit screen | Reported live, right after confirming #287's first fix — a second, different root cause in the same feature. Root cause: `saveEditMeal()` fires `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal` (async, fire-and-forget) then synchronously clears `editingMealId` — `MealEditScreen`'s own edit session immediately reads that as "done" and navigates back (`onFocusedMealDone` → `navigate(-1)`), unmounting the component before the toast's async lookup could ever set the old *local* `useState`. Fixed by lifting the toast out of local state into a new `useFastingWindowToastStore` (`hours`/`date`, in-memory only, no persistence — see the store file's own comment), scoped by date so a toast set while editing one day doesn't leak into an unrelated one after navigating elsewhere. `confirmDeleteMeal`'s #301 fix updated to call the store's `dismiss()` instead of a local setter. New regression test in `MealEditScreen.test.tsx` (asserts both the rendered toast text and the store state directly, since the latter is what actually survives the unmount). Live-verified via Playwright: added a time to an untimed meal via the dedicated edit screen, toast appeared on Today after navigating back. Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#302](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/302) | ✅ Done | Item macros don't wrap to their own row in view mode (any food, not just scanned) | Filed as reported with a screenshot, then clarified by the user: not scanned-item-specific — applies to any food item's view-mode row. Both duplicated item-row renderings found and fixed — `MealList.tsx`'s own view-mode item `<li>` and `DayDetail.tsx`'s near-identical copy (History/calendar-day panel) — each previously rendered name/kcal/amount/macros/emotion as one continuous inline text run inside a single element. Split into two `<p>` elements per item: name/kcal/amount on the first, macros + reaction emoji on a second, always-own-row paragraph underneath, same "own `<p>`" pattern the meal-group-level macros summary line already used one level up. No test asserted the old combined-string shape (checked before changing), so nothing needed updating beyond the fix itself. Live-verified via Playwright screenshot. Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |
| [#303](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/303) | ✅ Done | Let recipe ingredients be picked from the existing food list instead of manual entry only | Follow-up to #251. Added a "Find food" button to `RecipeEditorDialog`'s ingredient section, reusing `FoodPickerDialog` (the same curated/personal-food search every other add-food surface in the app already uses) rather than building a second picker. Same fallback-tier ordering the daily log's own add row established: search first, manual entry (already existed) as the fallback. Every checked dish (multi-pick already supported by the dialog) becomes its own ingredient directly — no draft-field round-trip, same "commits straight to the list" shape `addFoodToEditItems`/`addFoodEntry` already use elsewhere for this same dialog. No new i18n keys needed — reused `t.dailyEntry.addFoodButton`/`orDivider`, identical semantics to their existing use. New test in `RecipeEditorDialog.test.tsx`. Live-verified via Playwright (Settings → Manage recipes → Add recipe → Find food → picked Salmon → ingredient added with real macros). Full suite green. Confirmed by the user directly on-device |

## Tier 36 — iOS/Android native app store release (2026-07-23)

_The next big initiative, at the user's request: ship this PWA as installable native apps on the App Store and Play Store. Structured as one epic plus 14 focused child issues rather than a single giant checklist, matching how the rest of this repo's backlog works. Recommended approach (Capacitor, wrapping the existing Vite build rather than a rewrite) is documented in the epic; not yet implemented, filed for planning/sequencing only. **Reordered Android-first** (2026-07-23, at the user's request) — no developer accounts exist yet and the user is on Windows; iOS specifically requires a Mac to build/sign/submit (no way around it, Capacitor included), while Android needs only Android Studio (free, runs on Windows) and a one-time $25 fee vs. Apple's recurring $99/year. Getting-started checklist (accounts, tools, what to expect) saved outside this repo at `C:\Users\User\Projects\docs\turtle-steps-ideas\ios-android-release-checklist.md`, not duplicated here. Row order below is the new intended sequence, not issue-number order._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#304](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/304) | 🔲 Open | Epic: Ship iOS and Android native app store releases | Tracking issue only, links all 14 children below. Recommends Capacitor as the wrapping approach — reasoned default (fully local-first app, no backend/auth to re-point, Capacitor is the standard tool for exactly this "existing web app → native store presence" scenario), not yet locked in — see #305. Notes a valuable but out-of-scope follow-up: going native unlocks real push/local notifications for #171's daily reminder, previously closed as infeasible in #261 without a backend |
| [#305](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/305) | 🔲 Open | Adopt Capacitor to wrap the app for native iOS/Android builds | Foundational — everything else in this tier depends on it. `@capacitor/core`/`cli`/`ios`/`android`, `npx cap init` pointing `webDir` at the existing Vite `dist/`, generate both platform projects, verify the built app actually runs (incl. IndexedDB persisting across an app restart) — no signing/icons/store work yet |
| [#311](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/311) | 🔲 Open | Design an app icon and generate iOS/Android icon + splash-screen assets | Depends on #305. Blocks both store-submission chains — neither store accepts a submission without a finished icon. No dedicated app-icon artwork exists yet, only in-app UI; `@capacitor/assets` can generate every required size from one source image once designed |
| [#312](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/312) | ✅ Done | Write a privacy policy page (required by both app stores) | Confirmed on-device by the user. New standalone `PrivacyScreen.tsx` (`about/` feature folder) at its own stable `/privacy` route, lazy-loaded like every other non-Today route (`lazyRoutes.ts`). Covers what's collected (nothing automatic — only what the user enters), where it's stored (local device only, no account/server/cloud), sharing (none, no analytics/tracking), how data can leave the device (only via the user's own Settings export), children, policy-change, and contact sections, en+ru via a new `privacyPolicy` Dictionary block. `AboutScreen.tsx`'s existing one-line privacy blurb now links to it (`readPrivacyPolicyLabel`) rather than being replaced — the blurb stays as the at-a-glance summary, this page is the full policy the store listings need a URL for. Still needs the actual Data Safety (Play)/privacy nutrition label (App Store) questionnaires filled out per #317/#315 — this issue only covers the hosted page itself. |
| [#307](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/307) | 🔲 Open | Android: camera permission for barcode scanning inside the native shell | Depends on #305. `AndroidManifest.xml` permission plus Android's separate runtime-permission-prompt requirement (unlike iOS's Info.plist-string-only model) |
| [#308](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/308) | 🔲 Open | Native status bar / safe-area / system theming pass (iOS + Android) | Depends on #305. Covers both platforms in one issue, but moved up in sequence since Android ships first — Capacitor `StatusBar` plugin tracking the app's own light/dark + 5-color theme system; Android system nav bar; real-device audit of existing `env(safe-area-inset-*)` usage (a native full-screen WebView doesn't always compute these identically to a browser tab) |
| [#309](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/309) | 🔲 Open | Android: hardware/gesture back-button navigation handling | Depends on #305. `App.addListener('backButton', ...)` mapped to the same in-app back behavior `navigate(-1)`/`MealEditScreen.tsx`'s own back button already use, not a parallel navigation concept; only exits the app from a true top-level screen with nothing to go back to |
| [#310](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/310) | 🔲 Open | Verify/adjust the "update available" banner behavior inside the native shell | Depends on #305. The existing service worker/`AppUpdateBanner.tsx` flow assumes a hosted PWA where a refresh fetches a new deploy — inside a native app, updates come from the app stores instead, so this needs checking for conflicts/confusing copy, not assumed to just work |
| [#316](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/316) | 🔲 Open | Android: Google Play Console enrollment + app signing setup | Account/business step, not code. $25 one-time fee, upload keystore, Play App Signing |

| [#306](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/306) | 🔲 Open | iOS: camera permission for barcode scanning inside the native shell | Depends on #305. `NSCameraUsageDescription` in Info.plist (Apple rejects apps missing it); verify `getUserMedia` actually triggers the native permission prompt inside Capacitor's WKWebView, or whether Capacitor's own Camera plugin is needed instead |

## Tier 38 — Live feedback: make remaining-macro cards visual (2026-07-23)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |

| [#320](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/320) | ✅ Done | Add progress bar/ring to protein/fat/carbs/water remaining cards | Reported live with a screenshot: Today screen's 4 "remaining" StatCards (protein/fat/carbs/water) show only a bare number, no visual sense of proportion. An icon-grid idea (1 icon = fixed unit, e.g. 10g protein/fat/carbs, 250ml water) was discussed first via `AskUserQuestion`-style back-and-forth, then the user chose a progress bar/ring instead. First pass: `shared/ui/stat-card.tsx` gained `progressPercent`/`progressColor` props rendering one continuous fill, switching to solid `var(--foreground)` once at/over 100%, colored via the existing `--chart-protein`/`--chart-fat`/`--chart-carbs` tokens (water on `--primary`, no dedicated token). **Reopened on real-device feedback the same day**: the single continuous bar "looked boring," and the water card's over-goal `--foreground` fill read as unexplained black. Reworked into a **segmented** bar — `PROGRESS_SEGMENT_COUNT = 10` fixed equal-fraction segments, each solid `progressColor` once its own 10%-fraction is fully reached (`floor`, not `round` — a segment only lights up once truly earned) or a light `color-mix(in oklch, color, var(--card) 70%)` tint otherwise; at/over goal simply fills all 10 (no more special "over" color needed, the full segmented bar already reads as "done"). Also remapped colors per explicit request (protein=purple, fat=orange, carbs=green, water=blue) — since none of those match what `--chart-protein/fat/carbs` already mean on the Dashboard's macro chart, added 4 new dedicated, non-mood-tinted tokens (`--stat-protein/fat/carbs/water` in `index.css`, `:root`+`.dark` only, same "constant across every mood" precedent `--destructive` already sets) rather than repainting the existing chart tokens — each reuses an already-contrast-checked hue already in this palette (protein ← old `--chart-fat`, fat ← `--chart-calories`, carbs ← Sage mood's `--primary`, water ← old `--chart-carbs`) instead of an unaudited new color. New tests: `stat-card.test.tsx` covers exact segment-fill count and the all-filled at/over-goal case; `TodayScreen.test.tsx` covers wiring for protein (under goal) and water (over goal). Live-verified via Playwright in both light and dark mode: seeded a goal with all 4 targets plus a day consuming a mix of under/over amounts — screenshots confirm correct partial segment counts and the new purple/orange/green/blue colors in both themes |

## Tier 39 — Live feedback: show overage instead of clamping (2026-07-23)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#321](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/321) | ✅ Done | Show overage amount instead of clamping at 0 for fat/carbs/water remaining cards | Reported live: after drinking 3L against a 2L water goal, the card showed "0 ml left" — the extra liter is silently dropped, same clamping issue exists for fat/carbs. Protein already solved this shape (#266) with a positive "surplus — great job!" framing; ask is to extend the same over-target display to fat/carbs/water but with **neutral** wording (just "X g/ml over", no congratulatory copy), since going over isn't uniformly good for these three the way extra protein is. Implemented: `TodayScreen.tsx`'s `remainingFatG`/`remainingCarbG`/`remainingWaterMl` (all previously `Math.max(0, target - consumed)`) became signed `fatDeltaG`/`carbDeltaG`/`waterDeltaMl` (mirroring `proteinDeltaG`'s existing shape), each with an `isOverXTarget` flag; once over, the card shows `Math.abs(delta)` with a new `gOverUnit`/`mlOverUnit` unit instead of `gRemainingUnit`/`mlRemainingUnit`, keeping the same neutral `targetDenominatorText` description throughout (no protein-style congratulatory copy). `gOverProteinUnit` renamed to the now-shared `gOverUnit` (protein/fat/carbs all reuse it); new `mlOverUnit` added for water. `docs/issues-priority.md`/`Dictionary.ts` comments updated to match. New/updated tests in `TodayScreen.test.tsx` (fat and carbs each get a dedicated overage test, water's old "clamps at 0" test rewritten to assert the overage instead). Live-verified via Playwright with the user's own example (3L water vs 2L goal, plus fat/carbs pushed over their targets) — screenshot confirms "19 g over"/"51 g over"/"1,000 ml over", all fully-lit segmented bars, no congratulatory wording |
| [#322](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/322) | ✅ Done | Protein-vs-weight correlation: use protein % of calories instead of raw grams | Raised live: `ProteinCorrelationView`/`proteinCorrelation.ts` (#216) currently median-splits by raw protein grams, but that's confounded by total calorie intake — a high-protein day is usually just a high-everything day, so "more protein -> more weight gain" is largely picking up overeating in general, not a protein-specific effect. Ask: split on protein-as-%-of-total-calories instead, so a carnivore-style day (high protein share, normal total calories) reads differently from simply eating more of everything — stays consistent with every other correlation view's plain deterministic day-pairing/median-split, no real statistical modeling introduced. A more rigorous "only compare days with similar total calories" fix was discussed and deliberately deferred (shrinks the usable sample, bigger complexity jump). Implemented: `ProteinPoint.proteinG`/`ProteinCorrelation.thresholdProteinG` (`domain/stats/proteinCorrelation.ts`) became `proteinPercent`/`thresholdProteinPercent` — `proteinPoints()` now needs a day's `totalCalories` too (not just `totalProtein`), computing `(proteinG * 4) / kcal * 100`; a day with 0/undefined calories is skipped rather than dividing by zero. Threshold still rounds to a clean multiple (now of 5%, not 5g). `ProteinCorrelationView.tsx`'s scatter X-axis/tooltip switched from a gram amount to a new `proteinPercentOfCaloriesLabel` ("Protein (% of calories)") shown with `%` instead of `g`; `proteinCorrelationSummary`'s copy reworded from "ate less/more than Xg of protein" to "protein was less/more than X% of your calories" (en+ru). New domain tests assert the percent math directly and demonstrate the core motivating case: a bigger day (150g protein/3000kcal, 20% share) ranks *below* a smaller day with fewer raw grams but a higher share (80g/800kcal, 40%) — the whole point of the change. Existing fixtures across both test files updated from an arbitrary fixed `amountKcal: 100` (meaningless once protein is a % of calories) to a realistic `kcal` param. Live-verified via Playwright: seeded 8 comparable day-pairs split into a low-share cluster (8-11%) and a high-share cluster (40-48%) — screenshot confirms the scatter's X-axis now spans 0-60(%) and the summary reads "Days when protein was less than 25% of your calories averaged more weight gain..." |

## Tier 45 — Live feedback: dual y-axis for the comparison chart (2026-07-24)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#330](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/330) | ✅ Done | Compare chart: dual y-axis for exactly 2 selected series, hide axis for 3+ | Ask directly conflicted with `CustomChartView.tsx`'s own documented design: every series there is normalized to a shared, hidden 0-100 axis specifically because real per-series axes get unreadable past two or three. Since the ask only wants real axes for the *exactly-2* case (3+ stays exactly as before — already the existing behavior for any count), this is additive rather than a full reversal, but is still a genuine architectural fork over just an implementation detail. **Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`** before implementing (two options floated: real per-series axes for the 2-series case vs. a lighter-weight overlay of real tick labels on the unchanged normalized scale) — user chose the real-axes option. Implemented: new `isDualAxis = selectedNumeric.length === 2` in `CustomChartView.tsx`; when true, those two series' `Line`/`Bar` elements switch their `dataKey` from `${key}_norm` to the already-present `${key}_raw` (no changes needed to `customChartSeries.ts` — raw values were already carried in `data`, just unused for plotting before) and get `yAxisId="left"`/`"right"` matching two new visible `YAxis` elements (`width="auto"`, Recharts' default `'auto'` domain, tick color + axis-line color matching that series' own existing color, `tickFormatter` reusing each series' existing `formatRaw`). The always-present shared hidden axis (`yAxisId="normalized"`, unchanged `domain={[0,100]}` + `hide`) still carries boolean period/constipation markers regardless of dual-axis mode, and carries the numeric series too whenever `isDualAxis` is false (0, 1, or 3+ selected) — identical to before this issue. **Found before shipping, not after**: `customChartNormalizedCaveat` ("each line is scaled to its own range... doesn't represent an absolute value") is now factually wrong for the 2-series case, since real axes are absolute — hid that caveat paragraph specifically when `isDualAxis` is true, still shown for every other count. Live-verified via a seeded Playwright script (dev server + direct `indexedDB.open` seeding, since jsdom/vitest render zero Recharts SVG internals at 0×0 `ResponsiveContainer` dimensions — confirmed empirically, `.recharts-yAxis`/`.recharts-wrapper` queries return nothing in the existing test setup): 2 series shows both real axes in each series' own color/unit; 1 or 3+ shows neither, falling back to the existing normalized rendering. New `CustomChartView.test.tsx` tests cover the caveat-visibility branch (testable in jsdom, unlike the SVG axes themselves) for exactly-2 vs. 1 vs. 3 series. |

## Tier 46 — Live feedback: #330's dual axis starts weight at 0 instead of its minimum (2026-07-24)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#332](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/332) | ✅ Done | Compare chart: 2-series dual y-axis starts weight at 0 instead of its minimum | Follow-up to #330. Root cause: #330's two `YAxis` elements (`left`/`right`) left `domain` unset, so each fell back to Recharts' actual default of `[0, 'auto']` — forcing every axis's minimum to 0 regardless of the real data range, unlike the 3+ series case (`customChartPoints`' own min-max normalization) which visually starts from each series' minimum by construction. Fixed by giving both `YAxis` elements `domain={['auto', 'auto']}` — the same domain `WeightTrendChart.tsx` already uses for its own real weight axis — so each axis now scales from its own data's min to max instead of a hardcoded 0 floor. Live-verified via a seeded Playwright screenshot (weight 68–71.5 kg, water 800–4,000 ml): left axis now reads 68.0 kg at bottom (not 0.0 kg), right axis reads 800 ml at bottom (not 0 ml). **Also fixed in the same pass, at the user's request once spotted**: `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx` (#277) has its own independent exactly-2-series dual axis with the identical unset-`domain` bug, not something the user had reported — same fix applied there too (`domain={['auto', 'auto']}` on both axes), live-verified via a second seeded screenshot (muscle mass 31.0–33.2 kg, body fat 18.5–22.5%): axes now read 30.8/33.6 kg and 18/26% instead of starting at 0. |

## Tier 47 — Wearable/health-app data sources (2026-07-24)

_User asked about pulling data from Apple Health and Zepp Life. Researched via WebSearch before filing (neither has a public cloud API — both are on-device-only frameworks gated behind a native/hybrid mobile app), then confirmed scope with the user via `AskUserQuestion`: file the two integrations as correctly-scoped, blocked-until-mobile-app epics, plus a third, independently-buildable issue for importing manual data exports. A speculative fourth option (directly integrating Zepp's unofficial, undocumented API) was floated but **not** filed — the user didn't select it, and it would've needed a new backend component (this app is currently 100% client-side/IndexedDB) just to proxy credentials to an endpoint that could break at any time with no notice, for data Health Connect can already surface officially instead (see #335)._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#333](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/333) | 🚫 Split | Import weight/steps/etc. data from an Apple Health or Zepp Life export file | Closed, split into #365 and #366 below once real sample exports were obtained for both sources and turned out to need materially different technical approaches (AES-encrypted CSVs vs. a huge streamed XML file). |
| [#365](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/365) | ✅ Done | Import weight/body-composition data from a Zepp Life CSV export | Confirmed on-device. Split from #333. Implemented (2026-07-27): new "Import from Zepp Life" flow in Settings → Export, alongside the existing JSON backup import. `@zip.js/zip.js` (new dependency) decrypts the AES-encrypted export zip client-side using a password entered via a new dialog (never persisted); `src/features/export/zeppLife/` parses `BODY` (weight, body fat %, body water %, bone mass, visceral fat rating, muscle mass converted from Zepp's % to kg) and `ACTIVITY` (steps) CSVs and merges into `DailyEntry` by date — Zepp values win on a per-field conflict (user's choice), everything else on that day's entry untouched. `SLEEP` deliberately left out — its column units are still unconfirmed. Live-verified against the user's real export file via a seeded Playwright run: 207 days imported, correct weight values showing in History, zero console errors. |
| [#366](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/366) | ✅ Done | Import weight/steps/waist/water data from an Apple Health XML export | Confirmed on-device. Split from #333. Implemented (2026-07-28): new "Import from Apple Health" flow in Settings → Export, alongside the JSON backup and Zepp Life imports. Unencrypted zip (unlike Zepp Life's), so no password dialog — `@zip.js/zip.js` streams `export.xml` through a `WritableStream` sink instead of buffering the full ~1.3GB decompressed text. `src/features/export/appleHealth/` hand-rolled-chunk-scans `<Record/>` elements and maps `BodyMass`/`BodyFatPercentage` (HealthKit's declared `%` unit is actually a 0–1 fraction — non-obvious, confirmed from a real record)/`WaistCircumference` (only accepted when the record's own `unit` attribute says `cm`)/`StepCount` (summed per day)/`DietaryWater` (one `WaterEntry` per record) into `DailyEntry` patches, reusing #365's merge policy (imported value wins per field). `SLEEP` deliberately out of scope, same reasoning as #365. Generalized #365's Zepp-specific merge/timestamp-parsing code into shared `mergeDailyEntryPatches.ts`/`parseHealthTimestamp.ts`, fixing a real gap while at it — Apple's timestamps have a space before the UTC offset (`"...12:00:00 +0300"`), unlike Zepp's (`"...12:00:00+0000"`). **Found and fixed a genuine O(n²) bug during live verification**: a long `<Record>`-free stretch (inside a `Workout` block) let the scanner's buffer grow unbounded, and regex-matching an ever-larger accumulated string forced repeated re-flattening — 10+ minutes and still climbing against the user's real ~1.35GB export before the fix (actively bounding the buffer instead of just tracking a scan position), 14.6s in plain Node / 57s through the real browser UI after (2281 days imported, 0 console errors) — confirmed on the same real file both times. |
| [#334](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/334) | 📋 Not started | Epic — Apple Health integration (blocked on mobile app + HealthKit bridge) | HealthKit has no public/cloud API at all — data is on-device only, readable exclusively by a native iOS app (or hybrid app with a HealthKit plugin/entitlement) the user has granted permission to. Genuinely blocked until the mobile app exists; can't be built sooner by any workaround. |
| [#335](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/335) | 📋 Not started | Epic — Android Health Connect integration (blocked on mobile app) | Same category of blocker as #334 — Health Connect is Android's on-device health data store, no cloud API, native/hybrid-app-only access. Bonus: Zepp Life can itself sync into Health Connect, making this the official, supported route to Zepp/Amazfit data — likely preferable to ever building a direct, unofficial Zepp API integration. |
| [#337](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/337) | ✅ Done (researched, closed) | Investigate pulling sleep data from AutoSleep | Researched via WebSearch. AutoSleep is HealthKit-only for live/automatic data (reads/writes Apple's on-device HealthKit database, no cloud API, nothing transmitted off-device) — same blocked-until-mobile-app situation as #334, folds into it rather than needing separate tracking. Separately, AutoSleep has its own **manual CSV export** built into the app (Settings, date-range export) — a genuinely separate, buildable-now path the same shape as #333's Apple Health XML/Zepp Life CSV import, just a third file format. Flagged (not unilaterally scope-changed) as worth folding into #333 when that's implemented, rather than opening a fourth import-format issue. No code change — a pure research issue, closed with the finding as a comment. |

## Tier 48 — Custom correlations (2026-07-25)

_User asked for a way to correlate two things of their own choosing — including metrics the app doesn't track at all (their examples: training session vs. weight, acne vs. carbs consumption) — not just the existing fixed correlation pairs (#7, #116, #167, #216/#322)._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#336](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/336) | ✅ Done | Add custom correlations — user-defined metrics, data entry, and dashboard graphs | Confirmed on-device by the user — two follow-ups filed: [#362](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/362) (move value entry onto Today's log — reverses this issue's own "own dedicated screen" design fork, after actually using it) and [#363](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/363) (a free-text note per logged value, not just the bare number). **Five design forks resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before building** (the four the issue itself flagged, plus a fifth found while scoping): (1) value type — one numeric type underneath (`CustomMetricEntry.value`), three input widgets at define-time (plain number / yes-no toggle stored as 1-0 / 1-5 scale), not three separate domain types; (2) unlimited custom correlations, no cap; (3) data entry lives on its own dedicated screen, not folded into the daily log; (4) reuses the existing `domain/stats/*Correlation.ts` median-split shape — but (5), surfaced only once the "acne vs. carbs" example was checked against that shape literally: every existing correlation view is fixed as "metric vs. *next-day* weight change," which doesn't fit pairing two arbitrary metrics where *neither* side is weight, so a new **same-day** pairing engine was built alongside the existing next-day-delta one (additive, the 5 built-in views are untouched). New domain: `domain/customMetric/` (`CustomMetric`/`CustomMetricEntry`/`CustomCorrelation`/`MetricRef` — a discriminated union pointing at either a built-in `NumericSeriesKey` or a custom metric by id) and `domain/stats/customCorrelationEngine.ts` (`resolveMetricValueMap` reads either source into a plain `date -> value` map, `pointsFromValueMaps` intersects same-day pairs, `customCorrelationFromPoints` is the generalized median-split — same shape as `mealFrequencyCorrelationFromPoints` etc., just parameterized). Strength banding couldn't reuse the existing fixed-kg thresholds (those only make sense when the dependent axis is always weight) — new `classifyRelativeCorrelationStrength` bands the group difference against the B side's own standard deviation (Cohen's-d convention: medium=0.5/large=0.8 reused as this app's moderate/strong cutoffs, small folded into weak) so the same thresholds work whether B is kg, steps, or a 1-5 scale. New persistence: Dexie v11 adds `customMetrics`/`customMetricEntries` (unique `&[metricId+date]` compound index, same "one row per key" guarantee `dailyEntries`' own `&date` already backs)/`customCorrelations` tables + matching repositories, mirroring `Recipe`'s own "new table, no upgrade() needed" precedent. New `useCustomMetricStore`/`useCustomCorrelationStore` (mirrors `useRecipeStore`'s shape); `useCustomCorrelationStore.deleteCorrelationsReferencingMetric` cascades when a metric is deleted, since a correlation with one side pointing at nothing would otherwise dangle. New `CustomMetricsScreen.tsx` (reached from Settings, same "description + link button" card shape recipes/food-list use) — three sections: metric definitions, per-date value entry (own date-nav header mirroring `TodayScreen.tsx`'s prev/next-day arrows, per design fork #3), and correlation definitions (a plain native `<select>` per side spanning built-in + custom metrics — this app has no dedicated Select component and defining a correlation is rare enough not to warrant building one). New `CustomCorrelationView.tsx` on Dashboard — one card per defined correlation, reusing `useOutlierExclusion`/`OutlierPointsList`/`CorrelationStrengthLabel`/the same collapsed-until-there's-an-insight pattern the built-in views already use (`useOutlierExclusion` already took a generic string key, so no changes needed there). **Deliberate v1 scope trim, documented not silently skipped**: the custom-correlations list doesn't hook into `dashboardChartVisibilityStore`/`dashboardSectionOrderStore` — those are keyed by a fixed, known `DashboardChartKey` set, and retrofitting them for an unbounded user-defined list was judged bigger than this issue's core ask; deleting a correlation is this version's only "hide" mechanism. New export-schema fields (`customMetrics`/`customMetricEntries`/`customCorrelations`, all `.optional()` — same no-version-bump precedent recipes/mealItems/foodOverrides established, since these are purely new optional arrays an older bundle simply won't have) — import correctly resolves an existing `(metricId, date)` entry's id first before upserting, same `#207` ConstraintError-avoidance pattern `dailyEntries`/`mealItems` import already uses, since that pair has its own unique DB index. New tests: `customCorrelationEngine.test.ts`, `correlationStrength.test.ts` (relative classifier), `customChartSeries.test.ts` (`numericSeriesValueByDate`), `customMetricStore.test.ts`, `customCorrelationStore.test.ts`, `CustomMetricsScreen.test.tsx`, `CustomCorrelationView.test.tsx`, `exportBundle.test.ts`. Live-verified via Playwright against the dev server end to end (define both metric kinds, log values, define a correlation, confirm Dashboard renders without errors and correctly falls back to the existing empty state with zero daily entries). |

## Tier 49 — New observation: meal frequency vs. weight (2026-07-25)

_User asked (ru): "Корреляция что лучше, питаться три раза в день по 500 ккал или же пять раз по 300?" — is it better to eat 3x/day at ~500 kcal each or 5x/day at ~300 kcal each?_

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#338](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/338) | ✅ Done | Add a correlation: meal frequency (3x larger meals vs 5x smaller meals) vs. weight | Confirmed on-device by the user. Fits the existing hardcoded-correlation pattern directly (#116/#167) — meal count per day is already derivable from logged data (`entry.calorieEntries.length`, counting meal *groups* not items), so #336's generic custom-metric infra wasn't needed. New `domain/stats/mealFrequencyCorrelation.ts`, a close structural mirror of `lateMealCorrelation.ts` (#116): `mealFrequencyPoints(entries)` pairs each day's meal count with the *next* calendar day's weight delta (same two-endpoints requirement), `mealFrequencyCorrelationFromPoints(points)` median-splits into a fewer/larger-meals half and a more/smaller-meals half (`MIN_COMPARABLE_DAYS = 8`, same threshold), reporting which half averaged more next-day gain via the shared `classifyCorrelationStrength`. New `MealFrequencyCorrelationView.tsx`, a close mirror of `LateMealCorrelationView.tsx` — same collapsed-until-insight pattern, scatter chart (meal count on X instead of time-of-day), outlier exclusion via the existing `useOutlierExclusion` mechanism (own `'mealFrequency'` storage key), whole-card show/hide toggle. Wired into `DashboardScreen.tsx`'s `sectionsByKey`/`DEFAULT_DASHBOARD_SECTION_ORDER` and `dashboardChartVisibilityStore.ts`'s `DashboardChartKey` union, right after `lateMealCorrelation`. Distinct from #257 (meal *timing*) and #322 (meal *composition*) — this is meal *count*, a genuinely different dimension from either. New tests mirror `lateMealCorrelation.test.ts`/`LateMealCorrelationView.test.tsx` exactly, adapted for meal count instead of meal time, plus one extra case confirming a multi-dish meal counts once, not per item. |

## Tier 50 — Live feedback: past-goal rows don't show which weights reached the goal (2026-07-25)

_Reported live with a screenshot of the Goal screen's "Прошлые цели" table: two rows both read "Цель достигнута" + a date, with no weight numbers, so it's unclear which weigh-ins the achievement is based on._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#339](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/339) | ✅ Done | Show previous vs. current weight on past-goal rows (which weigh-ins reached the goal) | Confirmed on-device by the user. Read from the goal-evaluation logic's own internal comparison rather than re-deriving from the weight log — `goalWindowProgress()` (`domain/goal/goalWindowProgress.ts`) already computes a baseline weight (the weight logged on `weekStart`) to compare every day against; it just never returned it. Now returns `baselineWeightKg` plus a new `currentWeightKg` (the weight on `metOnDate` once the target was met, otherwise the most recently logged weight in the window) on `GoalWindowProgress`. `PastTargetsList.tsx`'s status cell gains a second, muted line ("80.0 → 78.5 kg") via a new `previousToCurrentWeightLabel` Dictionary entry (en+ru) — shown whenever a baseline weight is known, not only on "target met" rows, so a missed-target row also shows what it was actually judged against; omitted entirely for a row whose `weekStart` never got a logged weight (`targetMet`/`metOnDate` both still null). New tests in `goalWindowProgress.test.ts` (baseline/met-weight, fallback-to-latest-when-missed, undefined-until-baseline-known) and `PastTargetsList.test.tsx` (met + missed + omitted cases). |

_Reported live with two screenshots: a food ("Bio-skyr клубника-киви") just logged for lunch is buried mid-list, sorted alphabetically under "B", in the "Add from food list" search picker — instead of surfacing near the top like favorites already do._

|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#340](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/340) | ✅ Done | Recently added foods should sort near the top of the food picker, after favorites | Confirmed on-device by the user. "Recent" = last-logged (`MealItem.updatedAt`, already bumped every time `touch()` re-logs an item) — the reported item ("Bio-skyr клубника-киви") turned out to be a personal meal item, not a curated food, and curated foods have no per-user recency signal at all (`touchMealItem` deliberately skips them, `MealList.tsx`'s `curatedFoodNames` check) so extending this to curated picks was out of scope for what was actually reported. `FoodPickerDialog.tsx`'s `allMealItems` now sorts by `updatedAt` descending before merging with curated foods, replacing the alphabetical order it inherited from `mealItemRepository.getAll()` (left unchanged — Settings' own management list still wants alphabetical). No separate "top N recent, then fall back to alphabetical" cutoff needed: personal items already list ahead of every curated food structurally, so recency-sorting that block alone is enough. `sortFavoritesFirst`'s existing stable sort still applies on top, unaffected. New test asserts a more-recently-touched item renders above an older one. |

Every `MealItemEditorSheet`/`FoodPickerDialog`/`MealList.tsx` call site that already threaded protein/fat/carbs through (add-row, edit-item, "Find food," barcode-restore, autocomplete-restore, mode-switch conversions, `touchMealItem`) now threads fiber the same way. **Deliberate scope limits, documented rather than chased**: (1) `data/foods.ts`'s 300+ existing curated foods all have `fiber100: undefined` — backfilling real per-food fiber values for an already-authored list wasn't something to guess at, fabricated nutrition numbers in a health app would be actively wrong, not just incomplete; a personal item's own `lastFiberG` is unaffected by this. (2) `macrosSummaryText`/`macrosSummaryTextCompact` (the shared "P 20g · F 10g · C 5g" line rendered in meal-item rows, History's table, etc.) deliberately untouched — folding fiber in would have rippled into every one of those call sites; the Add Dish form's own live-total preview gets a separate, dedicated fiber line instead. (3) Recipes (`recipePerServing.ts`), the Settings food-list curated-override editor (`FoodListSettingsScreen.tsx`) and personal meal-item editor (`MealItemsSection.tsx`), barcode/Open Food Facts nutrient mapping (`lookupBarcode.ts`), Dashboard's `MacroTrendChart`/comparison-chart series, and `suggestDailyTargets`'s TDEE-based suggestion don't surface fiber at all yet — every one of these is a real, separately-scoped follow-up if wanted, same "staged rollout, not one big bang" precedent the original protein/fat/carb/water additions each followed. Found and fixed along the way, unrelated to fiber itself: `goalFormMapping.ts`'s `formValuesToGoal` fresh-record branch was missing `dailyFiberTargetG` mapping after an imprecise `replace_all` edit only caught the live-edit branch — caught by a failing `GoalForm.test.tsx` test before commit, not shipped. New tests: `totalMacros.test.ts`, `mealItemStore.test.ts`, `MealList.test.tsx` (add-row fiber save), `TodayScreen.test.tsx` (remaining-fiber card, neutral over-target framing), `GoalForm.test.tsx` (optional/submits/pre-fills, mirroring the carb target's own test shape). |

Missed during #344's mockup-review batch, which only cross-checked the food-picker's own checkbox multi-select against the new mockups, not this manual-entry capability. No release note (#131/#144 precedent — zero end-user-facing behavior change). |
| [#343](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/343) | ✅ Done | Add Steps and Sleep to Today's summary tiles, allow reordering | Confirmed on-device by the user — follow-up filed as [#353](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/353) (Sleep tile doesn't show deep sleep). **Scope resolved via `AskUserQuestion`**: the mockup's 6-tile compact grid layout would mean restructuring Today's whole top area — a bigger visual redesign that jumps ahead of the "one screen at a time" rollout agreed to start with Add Dish (#344), not Today. User chose the smaller option instead: two more plain `StatCard`s (Steps, Sleep — reusing `entry.steps`/`entry.sleepHours`, no target/remaining concept, same shape as the existing BMI card) added to Today's *existing* vertical-stack layout, made reorderable via the identical on-demand drag mechanism #297/#319 already gave Dashboard sections — not a full page-wide reorder system. ## Tier 53 — Live feedback: can't start today's log before the configured day-start time (2026-07-26)

_Reported live with a screenshot: at 09:12, the Today screen still showed 25 Jul with the forward arrow disabled, because Settings' day-start time (#298, closed) is set later than 09:12. #298 only covers the "up past midnight" case; there was no way to cross into the new day earlier than that fixed setting on a given morning without reconfiguring it (which would apply to every day, not just this one)._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#345](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/345) | ✅ Done | Let the user manually start today's log earlier than the configured day-start time | Confirmed on-device. One-tap affordance, chosen over the other candidates. `TodayScreen.tsx` computes a new `realTodayIso` (plain `format(new Date(), 'yyyy-MM-dd')`, ignoring `dayStartTime` entirely) alongside the existing `todayIso()` (#298's boundary-aware one) — whenever they diverge (the gap between midnight and the configured start time) *and* the user is currently viewing the effective "today" itself (not browsing an earlier day), a quiet bordered banner appears with a "Start today's log now" button that calls the existing `setDate(realTodayIso)`. Nothing persisted — `date` already lives in the URL search param (#200), so this is inherently a one-occasion jump: reloading later or coming back tomorrow goes back through `todayIso()` normally, and the banner disappears on its own once the real clock reaches `dayStartTime` (the two values converge with no user action needed). `dayStartTime` itself is completely untouched, satisfying the "without reconfiguring the boundary" ask. New tests cover: banner appears before the boundary, clicking it jumps the date field to the real day and hides the banner, banner absent once the boundary is reached, banner absent while viewing an earlier day. |

## Tier 55 — Live feedback: black chart color (2026-07-27)

_Reported live with a screenshot: the Overview "Comparison" chart's "часов голодания" (fasting hours) bar series renders in solid black, clashing with the app's warm beige/olive palette used everywhere else._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#347](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/347) | ✅ Done | Don't use black for chart series color (fasting-hours bar chart) | Confirmed on-device by the user. Not a missing mapping — `useNumericSeriesConfig` already gave fastingHours a color, `var(--chart-5)`, the last of five generic achromatic (zero-chroma) `--chart-1..5` slots shared with steps/waist/hip/bodyFat. Those work fine as thin *lines* (a deliberate lightness-graduated grayscale ramp for series with no dedicated identity color) but `--chart-5`'s own lightness (oklch L=0.269) is genuinely indistinguishable from black once filled solid as a *bar* — confirmed as the root cause via `CustomChartView.tsx`'s `<Bar fill={seriesConfig[key].color} .../>`, no opacity reduction. Fixed by giving fastingHours its own dedicated `--chart-fasting` token (a muted indigo, `#5f6a9e` light/`#a3aed9` dark, distinct from every other hue already claimed in this palette) instead of lightening the shared grayscale ramp — steps/waist/hip/bodyFat aren't reported as broken, and lightening `--chart-5` in place would just push the identical "looks black as a bar" problem onto whichever of them gets switched to bar mode next. `--chart-1..4` are unchanged. |

## Tier 58 — Live feedback while validating #347/#348 (2026-07-27)

_Reported live with two screenshots right after validating #347/#348 on-device: (1) the comparison chart still shows a solid black bar for a different series once "Процент жира"/body fat % is switched to bar mode; (2) "Талия"/"Бёдра" (waist/hip) still appear as selectable comparison-chart series even though "Измерения тела" (Body measurements) is turned off in Settings' "What to track" grid._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#350](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/350) | ✅ Done | Body fat % bar series still renders as black in the comparison chart | Confirmed on-device by the user. Root cause: `--chart-4` (`oklch(0.371 0 0)`), the second-darkest achromatic slot, was the root cause — same as #347's `--chart-5`. Fixed identically: `useNumericSeriesConfig()`'s `bodyFat` entry now uses a new dedicated `--chart-bodyfat` token (teal-forest green, `#3f7a5c` light/`#82c4a0` dark) instead of the shared generic slot. Checked waist/hip (`--chart-2`/`--chart-3`) as the issue suggested — left unchanged: their lightness (0.556/0.439) sits meaningfully closer to `--chart-1`'s already-fine medium gray than to `--chart-4`/`--chart-5`'s confirmed-too-dark end, and neither was actually reported. Not yet confirmed live on the deployed site by the user. |
| [#351](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/351) | ✅ Done | Waist/hip stay selectable in the comparison chart even when Body measurements tracking is disabled | Confirmed on-device by the user. Root cause confirmed and fixed. `CustomChartView.tsx` gained an `availableNumericKeys` filter (mirroring the existing `availableBooleanSeries` filter for period/constipation) — waist/hip now gate on `trackedFields.bodyMeasurements`, bodyFat gates on `trackedFields.bodyComposition` instead (not the same toggle — `DailyEntryForm.tsx` moved `bodyFatPercent`'s own form field into the "Body composition" section back in #263, even though it's stored on the same `DailyEntry` as waist/hip). This matches the user's exact report: they'd disabled "Измерения тела"/Body measurements but left "Состав тела"/Body composition on, so only waist/hip should've disappeared, not body fat — confirmed via `DailyEntryForm.tsx`'s own two separate `trackedFields.bodyMeasurements`/`trackedFields.bodyComposition` gates. An already-selected key isn't force-removed from the chart if tracking is turned off afterward — only what's offered going forward changes, same behavior the boolean series already have. New tests cover both toggles independently. |

## Tier 61 — Live feedback: Dashboard drag handle placement (2026-07-27)

_Reported live with a screenshot: in Dashboard's reorder mode, each section's drag handle renders on its own line above the whole section (title included) instead of next to the title text._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#355](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/355) | ✅ Done | Dashboard drag handle sits above the section title instead of beside it | Confirmed on-device by the user. Root cause: `SortableDashboardSection` rendered the grip-handle `<button>` itself, stacked above whatever `children` (one of 18 different section components) happened to render first — each section's own title text sat on the next line down, regardless of what that title's own markup looked like. Investigated (as flagged in the issue) whether this needed 18 separate per-file fixes or one shared fix — found that **all 18** sections already funnel their title through the one shared `ChartTitleWithToggle`/`SectionTitleWithToggle` pair (#245/#247), so the fix landed in one place: `SortableDashboardSection`'s `children` became a render-prop `(dragHandle: ReactNode) => ReactNode` instead of a plain element (`DashboardScreen.tsx`'s `sectionsByKey` map became a map of functions accordingly), and `SectionTitleWithToggle` gained a new `dragHandle` slot rendered immediately before its own `<h2>`, inside the same flex row — so the handle now sits inline with the title text instead of above the whole section. Every one of the 18 view components picked up the identical one-line change: a new optional `dragHandle?: ReactNode` prop, forwarded straight into that section's own existing `ChartTitleWithToggle` call. Live-verified via Playwright (grip icon now renders directly beside "Weight trend"/"Compare your data" on the same line). New test asserts the handle and its title share the same parent element. |
