|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#362](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/362) | ✅ Done | Move custom metric value entry onto Today's log instead of a dedicated screen | Confirmed on-device. Extracted the per-date value-entry list (`MetricValueRow` + surrounding section) out of `CustomMetricsScreen.tsx` into a new `CustomMetricLogSection.tsx`, mounted at the bottom of `TodayScreen.tsx` (after `DailyEntryForm`), using `TodayScreen`'s own currently-viewed `date` — so browsing to a past day via Today's own arrows also logs values for that day, not literally "today." Renders nothing at all if no metrics are defined, same "invisible until used" treatment as other optional Today sections. `CustomMetricsScreen.tsx` now only has metric definitions + correlation definitions, with a short note pointing to Today for logging. Fixed a real bug caught by the test suite: an initial `key={date}` on the new section collided with `DailyEntryForm`'s own identical `key={date}` on a sibling element, corrupting React's reconciliation (duplicate-key warning, duplicated DOM in tests) — removed, since the section has no local state of its own needing a remount (only `MetricValueRow` does, via its own already-correct per-metric key). |
| [#363](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/363) | ✅ Done | Add a note field per logged custom metric value | Confirmed on-device — but see #364, a follow-up filed live during this confirmation (no visible save action for the note field). New optional `CustomMetricEntry.note?: string` (purely additive, no Dexie version bump, added to `exportBundleSchema.ts` too). `customMetricStore.ts` gained `setEntryNote(metricId, date, note)` — amends an existing entry only (no-op with nothing to attach to, since `value` is required); `setEntryValue` now carries over an existing note instead of silently wiping it when just the value changes. `CustomMetricsScreen.tsx`'s `MetricValueRow` shows a plain text "Note" input below the value widget, only once a value for that day exists, committed on blur same as the number widget. Verified via a dedicated RTL test simulating the full flow (log a value → note field appears → type + blur → persisted with both fields) against real `fake-indexeddb` — the IndexedDB CLI seeding tool used for other issues' live screenshots hit a known store-count limitation here (documented in agent memory), so this automated coverage stood in for a live screenshot. |

## Tier 70 — Import from MyFitnessPal (2026-07-28)

_User asked directly whether MyFitnessPal data could be imported, right after #365/#366 shipped. Initially researched via WebSearch before a real sample existed (same diligence #333 originally got): assumed Premium/Premium+-only "Download Your Data" → a zip of CSVs. Corrected same day once the user obtained a real **"Data Access Request"** export and asked to inspect it — a different, unrelated mechanism producing one password-protected `.xlsx` (MS-OFFCRYPTO/OLE-wrapped, not a plain zip) with a single flat sheet keyed by an `item_type` column, not separate CSVs. Real sample: 2019-09-29 through today, 14,347 `Foods` rows (each carrying a `meal` field — Breakfast/Lunch/Dinner/Snacks — in `details_json`) plus `Measurement`/`Steps`/`Exercise`/etc. User's own stated priority: meals only — Steps/Exercise already come from Apple Health (#366)._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#367](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/367) | ✅ Done | Import calorie/meal and weight history from a MyFitnessPal export | Confirmed on-device by the user (including encrypted-export path from #500). Distinct payoff from #365/#366 (which only touch body metrics): `Foods` rows are the standout here, backfilling `CalorieEntry`/`CalorieItem` meal history via `(date, meal)` grouping, something neither of those two imports can do. `Measurement` rows (`description == "weight"`, `unit == "kilograms"`) give a clean `weightKg` mapping. Real export format confirmed once implementation started: a plain, unencrypted `.xlsx` (GDPR Data Access Request), not the password-protected MS-OFFCRYPTO zip the filing's notes had assumed from MyFitnessPal's other, Premium-only export mechanism — the decryption-library research question turned out moot entirely. User pasted the real 40-column header row directly rather than the column names being guessed. New `myFitnessPalParser.ts`/`importMyFitnessPal.ts`, same shape as the Zepp Life/Apple Health importers: reads via `exceljs` (already a dependency for the Excel *export* side) by column name, not position — row 1 title, row 2 header, data from row 3, an `item_type` discriminator column instead of one file per category. `Foods` rows group by `(date, details_json.meal)`; `details_json.brand_name` maps to `CalorieItem.brand` (#248, already existed). No `timeEaten` (every sampled row's `created_at`/`updated_at` were blank — a confirmed real gap, not guessed) and no `amountG` (the `value`/`unit` pair mixes a quantity with a serving-size unit that isn't reliably grams). `mergeDailyEntryPatches.ts` gained its first list-field merge: `calorieEntries` now appends onto whatever's already logged for that date instead of the existing scalar-only spread replacing it wholesale — resolved directly by the user (append, accept possible duplicates, no dedup requested). Wired into `ExportSection.tsx` alongside the other two importers. New tests across parser/importer/merge layers, plus a live Playwright round-trip against the real dev server (weight + 2 correctly-grouped meals landed in IndexedDB exactly as expected). `tsc -b`/lint/`myFitnessPalParser.test.ts`+`importMyFitnessPal.test.ts`+`mergeDailyEntryPatches.test.ts`+`ExportSection.test.tsx` green. Whether the Data Access Request path needs Premium is still unconfirmed, but doesn't block using it once obtained either way. Follow-up [#500](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/500) added password decrypt for the encrypted Premium export path. |

## Tier 71 — Apple Health / Zepp Life import follow-ups (2026-07-28)

_User asked to log three issues after #365/#366 shipped, none implemented yet. #337's finding (AutoSleep has no data of its own, just reads/writes HealthKit) is directly relevant to the first row below._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#368](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/368) | ✅ Done | Import sleep data from Apple Health export | Confirmed the open question from filing: `HKCategoryTypeIdentifierSleepAnalysis` is a Category-type record (stage-identifier string `value`, not a number — the actual amount is the `startDate`–`endDate` interval), handled as its own branch before the generic numeric-value check. Bucketed by the *wake* date. `Awake` excluded; `InBed` used only as a fallback when a source wrote no real `Asleep*` interval that night; `AsleepDeep` tracked separately as `deepSleepHours`. Same dominant-source-per-night pick as #385's `StepCount` fix, for the identical multi-source double-counting reason. HealthKit's exact category-value set implemented from stable public documentation, not confirmed against a real sleep export — flagged for on-device confirmation. `ImportFieldPicker`'s Apple Health field list grew from 5 to 7 fields; import blurb copy updated. Scoped to the Apple Health XML path only, matching the issue's own note — Zepp Life's CSV path not touched. New tests cover total/deep/awake-exclusion/InBed-fallback/cross-midnight-bucketing/dominant-source. `tsc -b`/lint/40 directly affected tests green; full suite run given this touches core parsing logic. |
| [#369](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/369) | ✅ Done | Let users pick which data types to import from Zepp Life / Apple Health | Closed on GitHub (docs had been left at Pending validation). New `ImportFieldPicker.tsx` (shared `ToggleGroup`-based multi-select chip picker, same pattern `CustomChartView`'s series picker already uses) — one instance per source in `ExportSection.tsx`, since Zepp Life (7 fields: weight/body fat/body water/bone mass/visceral fat/muscle mass/steps) and Apple Health (5 fields: weight/body fat/waist/steps/water) expose different data. All fields selected by default, matching pre-#369 "import everything" behavior exactly when untouched. New `filterPatchesToFields()` (`mergeDailyEntryPatches.ts`) restricts a patch map to the selected keys, dropping a date entirely once none of its fields survive (rather than upserting/touching an entry with nothing real to add) — both `importZeppLifeExport`/`importAppleHealthExport` gained an optional `includedFields` param, `undefined` (the default for any existing caller) meaning no filtering at all. Both "Import from X" buttons disable when their picker's selection is empty. New tests: `filterPatchesToFields` unit tests, an end-to-end test per importer confirming a filtered field is dropped and a real zip-decode still writes only the selected field, and `ExportSection.test.tsx` coverage for the picker's default-all-selected state and the disabled-when-empty button. |
| [#370](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/370) | ✅ Done | Let the JSON backup export respect the date-range picker too | **Reverses a prior decision on #240** (deliberately, resolved via `AskUserQuestion`): new, clearly separate "Export ranged backup" button below the period picker — the original "Export backup" button stays exactly as-is, always complete, the one guaranteed-complete restore source. New `handleExportRangedBackup()` filters `dailyEntries`/`customMetricEntries` (the date-scoped collections) by the period, same `filterByExportPeriod` helper Excel/CSV/Markdown already use (genericized from `DailyEntry[]`-only to `<T extends {date: string}>` to cover both). `goals` and every definition/reference collection (`mealItems`/`foodOverrides`/`recipes`/`customMetrics`/`customCorrelations`) stay complete regardless of the range, same scope Excel/CSV/Markdown already have. New tests cover the ranged export respecting the period (while keeping all goals) and exporting everything when no period is set. |

New Dictionary keys `howToExportLabel`/`howToExportSteps` on both `zeppLifeImport`/`appleHealthImport` (en/ru). New tests confirm each disclosure pairs the right toggle with the right steps. |

## Tier 73 — Live feedback: Dashboard doesn't scale to 6 years of data (2026-07-28)

_All reported live in one session, digging into Dashboard right after importing ~6 years of Apple Health data (#366). Several are different symptoms of the same underlying cause (charts/lists originally built and tested against a small dataset), so grouped together even though each needs its own fix._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#175](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/175) | ✅ Done | Navigation to Dashboard/History became very slow (10-20 seconds) | Confirmed on-device by the user: Dashboard now loads in about 4–5 seconds (was ~20 seconds). Originally closed on the reporter's own "no longer observing it" confirmation, no code change — reopened per its own closing note ("reopen with fresh details if it recurs"). This time there's a real plausible cause the original report didn't have: ~6 years of imported data (#365/#366), which the original filing's own suspects (`IndexedDbDailyEntryRepository.getAll()` full scan, per-chart recomputation) had been dismissed for lack of data volume. User's suggested fix: caching — not committed to yet, needed profiling first to find the actual bottleneck. Profiled with a seeded 2,190-day dataset: reproduced and *worse* than filed (170+ seconds, not 10-20), Chrome DevTools Protocol CPU profile showed almost all self-time inside `parseISO`/`differenceInCalendarDays` — `rollingAverage()` (`domain/stats/rollingAverage.ts`, used by `WeightTrendChart`/`CalorieTrendChart`) re-filtered and re-parsed every entry's date for every distinct date, an O(n²) shape (~9.6M date-fns calls combined at this data size). Rewritten as a single O(n) sliding window over the already-sorted entries. Re-profiled against the identical seeded dataset: 170s → ~9-11s in Vite dev mode, **~4.3s against an actual production build** (dev mode's React StrictMode/diagnostics meaningfully inflate the gap — checked deliberately, since the profile alone would have overstated the real user-facing cost). History was already fast (<1s in isolation) — an apparent 14s reading during back-to-back navigation was contention from the just-finished Dashboard load bleeding over, not a real History cost. `IndexedDbDailyEntryRepository.getAll()` itself confirmed *not* the bottleneck — no caching layer needed. New regression test asserts roughly-linear (not quadratic) scaling with entry count. `tsc -b`/lint/`rollingAverage.test.ts` (9 tests) green. |
| [#372](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/372) | ✅ Done | Navigate to a flagged outlier's day from a correlation view | Resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: a small separate link icon next to each chip, existing tap-to-exclude behavior completely untouched. `OutlierPointsList.tsx` gained a required `getDate` prop (separate from `getKey`/`formatLabel`, since `CorrelationView`'s weekly points key/label by `weekStart`, not a single day, but still have a sensible date to navigate to) and an `ArrowUpRight` icon-only `Link` to `/history?date=X`, reusing the same route `BodyCompositionTrendChart`'s tooltip link already targets. All 8 call sites updated (7 pass `point.date`, `CorrelationView` passes `point.weekStart`). Adding `<Link>` meant every one of these 8 test files needed a `MemoryRouter` wrapper on their `render()` calls (none had one before, since nothing in these components used routing until now) — fixed via a small script for the 7 files with uniform single-line render calls, by hand for the 2 with multi-line/different-shaped calls. New representative tests (one `date`-based view, one `weekStart`-based) assert the link's href. Full dashboard suite green (21 files/177 tests). |
| [#374](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/374) | ✅ Done | Outlier date-chip list needs a year and pagination/scroll cap | Chose the scrollable-container option flagged in the issue over pagination (smaller change, no new interactive state). `OutlierPointsList.tsx`'s chip container gained `max-h-40 overflow-y-auto`. All 8 call sites (`CorrelationView`/`CustomCorrelationView`/`SleepCorrelationView`/`ProteinCorrelationView`/`FastingWindowCorrelationView`/`StepsCorrelationView`/`MealFrequencyCorrelationView`/`LateMealCorrelationView`) shared the identical `formatLabel` pattern (`'d MMM'`, no year) — changed all 8 to `'d MMM yyyy'`. Existing tests across all 8 asserted the old no-year button names verbatim; updated to match. |
| [#373](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/373) | ✅ Done | Weekly/monthly summary cards don't show the year in their date range | Turned out to be weekly-only: `MonthlySummaryCards.tsx`'s `'MMMM yyyy'` format already included the year, `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx`'s `'MMM d'` didn't. Fixed by adding `, yyyy` to both the week-start and week-end `format()` calls in `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx` only — deliberately not touching the shared `t.dashboard.weekRange`/`t.common.weekRangeLabel` template functions or their other call sites (`MetTargetList`/`PastTargetsList`/`GoalScreen`/`TodayScreen`), which each format their own strings and only ever show a single current/recent week, not a multi-year list. New test asserts the year renders. |
| [#378](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/378) | ✅ Done | Trend/compare chart x-axis labels omit the year, unreadable across multi-year data | Confirmed: all 5 charts (`WeightTrendChart`/`CalorieTrendChart`/`MacroTrendChart`/`BodyCompositionTrendChart`/`CustomChartView`) share the identical pattern — the tooltip already used `'PP'` (locale-aware, year-inclusive) but each `XAxis`'s own `tickFormatter` used `'MMM d'` with no year. Changed all 5 to `'MMM d, yyyy'`, matching #373's chosen format. No new automated test: this codebase's existing precedent (#330/#332/#348) already established Recharts renders zero SVG under jsdom's `ResponsiveContainer`, so axis tick output has no existing or addable jsdom coverage — verified by code inspection (identical `format()` call already covered by #373's test, just relocated into a `tickFormatter` prop) rather than a live screenshot. |
| [#379](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/379) | ✅ Done | Add pagination/scroll cap to the Weekly Summary cards | Same `max-h-96 overflow-y-auto` scroll-container treatment as #374's outlier chip lists — chosen over pagination for the same reason (smaller change, no new interactive state). Applied to both `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx` and `MonthlySummaryCards.tsx` (confirmed it has the same unbounded-list gap, same fix). |
| [#375](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/375) | ✅ Done | Steps-vs-weight correlation chart's x-axis is stretched unreadable by a couple extreme values | Implemented your chosen fix (auto-clip to a percentile-equivalent range): `StepsCorrelationView.tsx`'s x-axis domain now reuses `outlierBounds()` (Tukey's-fences, the same bounds already deciding which points render red/get listed below, #224) instead of Recharts' raw min/max `'auto'` domain — `domain={[0, stepsBounds.upper]}` + `allowDataOverflow`, falling back to `[0, 'auto']` exactly as before when there's too little data (<4 points) for bounds to mean anything. **Also confirmed the suspected data-quality bug, filed separately as #385**: `appleHealthParser.ts`'s `StepCount` case sums every record for a date with zero deduplication by `sourceName` — a real, not speculative, double-counting bug whenever iPhone + Apple Watch both log the same walk. No new component test (chart-axis-domain rendering has no jsdom coverage, same established precedent as #330/#332/#348); the underlying `outlierBounds()` math already has direct unit tests. |
| [#376](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/376) | ✅ Done | Body composition chart shows a mostly-empty date range when a metric's data is sparse | Root cause confirmed: `bodyCompositionPoints()` returns one row per date with *any* of the 5 fields logged, and the chart's `data` used that full set unfiltered regardless of which series were actually selected. Fixed by narrowing `data` to `visiblePoints` (dates where at least one *currently-selected* series has a value), computed after the series picker's selection — deliberately left the existing `MIN_TREND_DATA_POINTS`/"not enough data" gate based on the *full* dataset untouched (a selection choice narrowing the visible range isn't the same as too little data existing at all; an earlier attempt that conflated the two broke the existing "deselect one at a time" test, reverted). **Found and filed separately, not fixed here**: #384 — `CustomChartView`'s "Compare data" chart shares the identical root cause (`customChartPoints()` uses the full unfiltered entry list), a broader version of this bug, but has its own added complexity (boolean markers, #330/#332's dual-axis case) that deserves its own pass. |
| [#380](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/380) | ✅ Done | Let users pick the trend chart's time period (week/month/year/custom) | Resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: one global control, mirroring #240's shape. New `domain/stats/trendChartPeriod.ts` (`resolveTrendChartPeriodRange`/`filterEntriesByTrendChartPeriod`, rolling windows anchored to today, not calendar-aligned — "last 7/30/365 days," not "this calendar week/month/year"), new persisted `useDashboardPeriodStore` (`'all'` default, matching every chart's pre-#380 behavior exactly), new `DashboardPeriodPicker.tsx` (5-option single-select: All time/Week/Month/Year/Custom, date inputs only shown for Custom). **Scoped to just the 4 main trend charts** (Weight/Calorie/Macro/Body composition) — deliberately excludes the 6 correlation views (their day/week-count caveats describe a full-history sample; narrowing the input would silently change what the statistic means, a bigger change than this issue asked for) and `CustomChartView` (`#384` already tracks that component's own different-shaped gap separately). `DashboardScreen.tsx` computes one filtered `trendChartEntries` array reused by all 4 chart props; every other section keeps reading the original unfiltered `entries`. New tests: `trendChartPeriod.test.ts` (pure range/filter logic), `DashboardPeriodPicker.test.tsx` (picker UI), and a `DashboardScreen.test.tsx` integration test proving the scoping — switching to "Week" drops the Weight trend chart to "not enough data" while Weekly summary still shows a year-old week untouched. Full test suite green. |
| [#377](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/377) | ✅ Done | Let users delete logged data for a specific date range | Scope resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: "daily entries + everything tied to those dates" over "just daily entries" — `CustomMetricEntry` is the one other collection with its own `date` field, so it's the only other one in scope; every definition/reference collection (goals, mealItems, foodOverrides, recipes, customMetrics, customCorrelations) stays untouched, same reasoning #370/#240 already established for what a date range does and doesn't touch. New `DeleteRangeSection.tsx` (Settings, right before `ClearAllDataSection` in the same end-of-page destructive-actions group) — a date-range picker (both dates required, unlike the export period's "blank means everything") plus a two-step confirm showing the *actual row count* about to be deleted (computed before confirming, not guessed), and a distinct "nothing to delete" message instead of a pointless zero-count confirm prompt. Reuses `IndexedDbDailyEntryRepository.getRange()` (already existed) and a plain JS filter for `CustomMetricEntry` (no range index there, same approach #370's ranged export already uses). New test file covers the disabled-until-both-dates state, the count-specific prompt (with/without custom metric logs), the nothing-to-delete case, cancel, and the actual deletion (confirms out-of-range data and goals survive). |

| [#382](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/382) | ✅ Closed, not reproduced | New goal shows "target met" on a date before the goal existed | Root cause confirmed: not a bug in `goalWindowProgress()`'s invariant (metOnDate ≥ weekStart always held), but a collision with #181's own deliberate "editing a still-live window keeps its weekStart" design — saving on Jul 28 silently kept a still-running Jul 24 window (self-consistent, since Jul 25 ≥ Jul 24), just not what the user meant by "setting a new goal." **Resolved via `AskUserQuestion` + a follow-up compromise from the user**: rather than always resetting (reversing #181 outright) or only making the existing behavior visible, `GoalForm` now offers an explicit second button, "Start a new goal," alongside the normal Update, shown only while `isEditingLiveWindow` (now exported from `goalFormMapping.ts`) would otherwise auto-resolve to edit-in-place. Clicking it passes a new `forceNew` flag through to `formValuesToGoal`, bypassing the automatic check and always stamping a fresh `weekStart = today`. New Dictionary keys `startNewGoalButton`/`startNewGoalHint` (en/ru). New tests cover the button's visibility (live+unreached → shown; reached or window-ended → hidden) and that it forces a fresh record. Full suite green (137 files/1375 tests). |

## Tier 75 — Night eating tracking (2026-07-28)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#383](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/383) | ✅ Done | Track night eating (yes/no, moon icon), include in correlations | Design fully resolved via `AskUserQuestion` (multiple rounds): dedicated built-in field + its own correlation view (not the generic custom-metric route) — kept as its own view despite #116's `LateMealCorrelationView` already correlating last-meal-time with next-day weight via median split, since a fixed yes/no cutoff is a different, more concrete question. Data source is a hybrid: new `DailyEntry.nightEatingOverride?: boolean` layered on a value derived from existing `timeEaten` data (any meal at/after a fixed 21:00 cutoff), via new `domain/dailyEntry/nightEating.ts`'s `hadNightEating()` — `undefined` means "use the derived value," `true`/`false` always wins once set. Unlike onPeriod/hadConstipation, **not** gated behind a Settings opt-in — always on, since anyone already logging meal times needs no extra step. Wired everywhere those two already appear: a `Moon`-icon toggle on both `DailyEntryForm.tsx` (Today) and `DayDetail.tsx` (History), a 4th always-on calendar dot (`bg-indigo-500`) in `CalendarView.tsx`, a 3rd `BOOLEAN_SERIES` entry in `CustomChartView.tsx`'s Compare Data chart (also always offered, no tracking-store gate), and a new `NightEatingCorrelationView.tsx`. That last one is the one genuinely new shape: since the predictor is already boolean, new `domain/stats/nightEatingCorrelation.ts` does a plain two-group comparison (night-eating days vs. not) rather than every other correlation view's median split — kept as its own view despite the conceptual overlap with #116, confirmed with the user. Its scatter chart plots two fixed columns (x=0/"No", x=1/"Yes") rather than a continuous scale. Registered in `dashboardChartVisibilityStore.ts`/`dashboardSectionOrderStore.ts` like every other Dashboard section. New i18n keys throughout (`dailyEntry.nightEating*`, `dashboard.nightEatingCorrelation*`), en/ru. Found and fixed one pre-existing gap along the way: `dailyEntryFormSchema.ts`/`dailyEntryFormMapping.ts` needed the new field added explicitly (react-hook-form doesn't persist a watched-but-unregistered field through `getValues()`) — caught by a failing test before it could ship broken. Also added a `nightEatingColumn` to the CSV/XLSX/Markdown exports (`exportCsv.ts`/`exportXlsx.ts`/`exportMarkdown.ts`, per the user's follow-up ask) — unlike onPeriod/hadConstipation's raw optional field, this column exports the *effective* `hadNightEating()` value, since night eating has no untracked/opt-in state to leave blank. Full test suite run (143 files/1449 tests green before this export addition; export test files re-run green after). |

Replaced with a `relevantDates` set — union of (dates where at least one *selected* numeric series has a value) ∪ (selected boolean markers' own flagged dates) ∪ (selected custom metrics' own logged dates) — so the chart's x-axis only spans where the current selection actually has something to draw. No new component test: same established precedent as #375/#378 — Recharts renders nothing under jsdom's 0×0 `ResponsiveContainer`, so the `data` array's actual date range has no addable jsdom coverage; verified by code inspection instead. ## Tier 77 — Confirmed Apple Health step double-counting (2026-07-28)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#385](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/385) | ✅ Done | Apple Health step import likely double-counts multi-source overlapping steps | Resolved via `AskUserQuestion` given no real multi-source sample export to validate a true interval-overlap merge against: best-effort per-day dominant source, not full interval merging. `AppleHealthRecord` now captures `sourceName` (parsed but previously discarded). `AppleHealthPatchBuilder` sums same-source intraday `StepCount` records same as before, but now keyed per `(date, sourceName)`; `build()` keeps only the single source with the highest total for that date and discards the rest, rather than summing across sources. A record with no `sourceName` at all (not expected in a real export) falls into its own single "unknown" bucket rather than silently merging into whichever named source is seen first. Known limitation, accepted as part of the chosen approach: a day where two sources each captured different real activity (e.g. phone left at home part of the day) could undercount rather than double-count — the safer direction of the two possible errors, and the same trade-off #368's own research already flagged for HealthKit multi-source data in general. New tests cover dominant-source selection, per-date independence, and that same-source summing is unchanged. |

## Tier 78 — Live feedback: goal CTA confusion, fasting-window day bug, drag handle (2026-07-28)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#388](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/388) | ✅ Done | Today screen's stat-card drag handle sits above the card, not to its left | Confirmed: `TodayScreen.tsx`'s `SortableTodayCard` rendered the grip handle above `children` inside a `flex flex-col` wrapper (unlike Dashboard's own #355 fix, these StatCards have no title row for the handle to slot into via a render-prop). Changed the wrapper to `flex items-center` with the handle as a `shrink-0` first child and `children` wrapped in a `min-w-0 flex-1` sibling — a column layout instead of a stacked one. No visual/behavioral change outside reorder mode (single full-width child either way). Verified via `tsc -b`/full `TodayScreen.test.tsx` (77 tests, unaffected — no test asserted the handle's layout classes). |
| [#386](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/386) | ✅ Closed, not reproduced | Today screen shows a false "Goal achieved" card right after starting a new goal | Root cause wasn't a math bug in `goalWindowProgress()` (confirmed architecturally impossible for a genuinely fresh `weekStart=today` record to show `metOnDate` non-null on day one) — it was the #181/#155/#382 auto-detection itself: a plain "Update" click silently decided, from internal reached/live-window state invisible to the user, whether it edited the current goal in place or quietly started a fresh one. Confirmed live with the user: their only available action was a single Edit pencil (no distinct "create new" affordance), and what they believed was "setting a new goal" had actually kept editing an older, already-past-its-target window in place. **Resolved via explicit user design direction, not `AskUserQuestion`**: "I want a separate CTA for creating a new goal... One CTA to edit existing goal. Another CTA to create new one. Creating new goal should close the existing goal." Implemented as a full redesign: `formValuesToGoal(values, unit, existingGoal, startNew)` — `startNew` is now the caller's own explicit choice, not auto-derived from `isEditingLiveWindow`/`activeGoalReached` (both removed entirely). `GoalForm`'s collapsed summary view now shows two always-visible actions side by side — the pencil "Edit" (always edits `existingGoal` in place, unconditionally, even once reached or expired) and "Start a new goal" (reused #382's own button copy/hint, now unconditional rather than gated behind the live+unreached case; always creates a fresh record). "Closing" the old goal needed no new status field — `pastGoals()`/`getActiveGoal()` already treat "every goal except the newest by `createdAt`" as closed, so a fresh record's later `createdAt` naturally supersedes it. Rewrote `GoalForm.test.tsx`'s/`goalFormMapping.test.ts`'s/`GoalScreen.test.tsx`'s old conditional-visibility tests to match (96 tests total, green) plus a live Playwright check confirming the submit button correctly relabels to "Set" after clicking "Start a new goal." |
| [#387](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/387) | ✅ Done | Fasting window calculation is off by a day for a past-midnight meal | Root cause confirmed (user has a custom day-start time set in Settings, #298): a meal logged before that cutoff gets filed under the *previous* calendar day's own `DailyEntry` (`effectiveDateFor`), so a real past-midnight meal (e.g. "01:22") ends up sitting alongside that day's own evening meal (e.g. "19:41") in the same record. `fastingWindow.ts`'s `lastMealTimeMinutes`/`earliestMealTimeMinutes` compared raw clock-minutes, so `Math.max` picked 19:41 as "the last meal" instead of the actually-later 01:22 (past real midnight) — exactly matching the reported 17.9h instead of the true ~12.2h gap. This was a known, deliberately deferred gap from #298 itself (its own scope note: "streaks, weekly/monthly summaries, correlation day-pairing, and the fasting-window toast are unaffected for now"), now actually hit by a real user. New `adjustForDayStart()` shifts any time before the cutoff forward by 24h before comparing, restoring its true chronological position. `fastingHoursBetween`/`lastMealTimeMinutes`/`earliestMealTimeMinutes` take an optional `dayStartTime` (default `'00:00'`, i.e. today's exact existing behavior) — scoped to just the reported toast (`MealList.tsx`, now reading the real `useDayStartStore` value); `fastingWindowCorrelation`/`customChartSeries.ts`'s `fastingHours` series deliberately still default to midnight, same already-acknowledged deferred scope as #298, not silently changed here. New tests reproduce the exact reported scenario (17.9h without the fix, ~12.2h with it) plus a full `MealList.test.tsx` toast test. |

| [#392](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/392) | ✅ Done | History's "weeks goal met" list is missing the year, same class as #373 | Confirmed: `MetTargetList.tsx` was explicitly excluded by #373's reasoning ("only ever shows one recent week"), but it actually renders a full historical list like `WeeklySummaryCards` — that reasoning didn't hold. Checked `PastTargetsList`/`GoalScreen`/`TodayScreen` per the issue's own note: `PastTargetsList.tsx` has the identical real gap (a full historical list of past goals, 4 more `'MMM d'` call sites fixed the same way); `GoalScreen.tsx`/`TodayScreen.tsx` only ever show the *current* goal's single week and `LoggingConsistencyHeatmap.tsx` is bounded to `MAX_DISPLAYED_WEEKS` — #373's reasoning genuinely does hold for those three, left unchanged. All changed to `'MMM d, yyyy'`, same format #373/#378 chose. Updated `PastTargetsList.test.tsx`'s now-outdated no-year assertions (5 tests) to match; `tsc -b`/lint/both affected test files (14 tests) green. |
| [#393](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/393) | ✅ Done | Compare Data chart shows no y-axis when exactly 1 series is selected, same class as #348 | First attempt (`isSingleAxis`) was confirmed not fixed on-device. Root-caused this time via a real seeded Playwright run (not jsdom, which renders zero Recharts SVG internals): the axis was rendering with negative x-coordinates, off-canvas. Cause: the hidden `normalized` axis defaults to the same `orientation="left"` as the new "single" axis, confusing Recharts' auto-width margin expansion. Fixed by giving the hidden axis `orientation="right"` instead — verified live (positive coordinates, visible), dual-axis + boolean markers confirmed unaffected. `tsc -b`/lint/full dashboard suite (197 tests) green. |
| [#394](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/394) | ✅ Done | Night eating correlation silently defaults untracked days to "No", inflating sample size | `hadNightEating()` widened to `boolean \| undefined` (undefined = no override and no meal that day has any `timeEaten`); `nightEatingPoints()` now skips those days entirely instead of folding them into "No." Every other consumer (toggle UI, calendar dot, chart marker, CSV/XLSX/Markdown exports) already degraded gracefully to "don't show"/blank with zero code changes needed, confirmed via `tsc -b` — only `DailyEntryForm.tsx`'s two-option toggle needed a real fix (now shows neither Yes/No pressed instead of defaulting to No). Every test asserting the old default-to-false behavior as correct was itself testing the bug — updated across `nightEating.test.ts`/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`/`DayDetail.test.tsx`/all 3 export test files; new `nightEatingCorrelation.test.ts` case reproduces the report's exact shape. `tsc -b`/lint/9 directly affected test files (180 tests) green; full suite run given the breadth. |
| [#395](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/395) | ✅ Done | Weekly/Monthly Summary cards render blank except the date label | First attempt (`will-change-transform`, WebKit-only theory) confirmed not fixed. Real root-caused this time via live DevTools on the user's own broken case, reproduced in plain Chromium (ruling out Safari-specific): `Card`'s base `overflow-hidden` makes a flex item's automatic minimum size resolve to 0 per spec (instead of content-based), letting a `StatCard` shrink below its content height inside the `max-h-96` scrollable list, with `overflow-hidden` then clipping the overflow. Fixed with `overflow-visible` at the `StatCard` level (safe everywhere — it never renders an image). Removed the now-confirmed-ineffective `will-change-transform`. `tsc -b`/lint/374 affected tests green. |
| [#396](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/396) | ✅ Done | Extend the Dashboard time-period picker to correlation views and Compare Data chart | Resolved the fork via `AskUserQuestion`: apply everywhere, accept the smaller-sample trade-off for correlations too. `trendChartEntries` renamed to `periodFilteredEntries`, wired into `CustomChartView` + all 8 correlation views. New test confirms a correlation view now disappears under a narrowed period, while `WeeklySummaryCards` (outside this issue's scope) stays unaffected. `tsc -b`/lint/all 23 dashboard test files (196 tests) green; full suite run given the breadth. |
| [#397](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/397) | ✅ Done | Chart date labels read backwards in Russian (month-day-year instead of day-month-year) | Confirmed `date-fns`'s `'PP'` preset (already used in `WeightTrendChart.tsx`'s tooltip) produces byte-identical English output to the literal `'MMM d, yyyy'` string while correctly reordering for Russian — switched every literal `'MMM d, yyyy'`/`'MMM d'` call site to `'PP'`: all 5 trend charts + `CustomChartView.tsx` (#378's own files), `MetTargetList.tsx`/`PastTargetsList.tsx` (#392's), `WeeklySummaryCards.tsx` (#373's), plus `GoalScreen.tsx`/`TodayScreen.tsx` (same order bug found during this pass, not previously reported since they don't need a year — fixed for order anyway). `LoggingConsistencyHeatmap.tsx`'s compact per-row label has the identical bug but was deliberately left unfixed — no room for `'PP'`'s year suffix in its `text-[10px] whitespace-nowrap` layout, needs its own locale-conditional short token, not reported live. Updated 3 test files' (`GoalScreen.test.tsx`/`TodayScreen.test.tsx`/`PastTargetsList.test.tsx`, already touched by #392) now-outdated assertions to match. `tsc -b`/lint/all 10 directly affected test files (169 tests) green; full suite run to confirm nothing else broke given the breadth. |
| [#398](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/398) | ✅ Done | Use gender-correct Russian verb form for the night-eating label when Profile sex is set | `Dictionary.nightEatingLabel` changed from a plain string to `(sex?: Sex) => string`, same pattern `mealLabel(n)` already established. `ru.ts` picks "Ела"/"Ел"/the neutral "Ел(а)" placeholder by `sex`; `en.ts` ignores the param. `DailyEntryForm.tsx`/`DayDetail.tsx` pass the real `useProfileStore().sex`; `CustomChartView.tsx`/`NightEatingCorrelationView.tsx` call with no argument (generic label regardless of viewer). `tsc -b`/lint/5 affected test files (175 tests) all green. |
| [#399](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/399) | ✅ Done | Show remaining calories for today while adding food (barcode scan / Find food / Add food) | Found existing infra to extend rather than build fresh: `todayTotalPreview`/`todayTotals` (#260/#273/#278) already showed a running-total preview in `MealItemEditorSheet` (covers manual entry + barcode scan, same sheet) and `FoodPickerDialog` ("Find food") — just absolute totals, not remaining-against-target. New sibling `todayRemainingWouldBeLabel` preview line, shown only when the goal has a `dailyCalorieTargetKcal` set, computed the same way as the existing preview. `dailyCalorieTargetKcal` threaded down via `useGoalStore` in `DailyEntryForm.tsx`/`DayDetail.tsx` (goal already loaded by an ancestor in both cases) through `MealList.tsx`. `MealEditScreen.tsx` deliberately not wired — its focused-edit mode has no add-row to attach to. ## Tier 80 — Retroactive: another flaky-test fix filed after the fact (2026-07-29)

_Caught by a background full-suite run after #396 shipped — filed before committing this time, unlike Tier 57/59's own retroactive fixes._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#400](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/400) | ✅ Done | GoalForm test flaky against a real midnight rollover during full-suite runs | `const today = new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` (captured once at test start) and the code under test's own `new Date()` (called on submit, after several real async interactions) landed on different calendar days once a real midnight rollover happened mid-run — a genuine race against the wall clock, not app-code-dependent flakiness. Fixed with `vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ['Date'] })`/`vi.setSystemTime(...)` + `userEvent.setup({ delay: null })`, same pattern `TodayScreen.test.tsx`'s day-start-time tests already established; `vi.useRealTimers()` added to the file's existing `afterEach`. No release note (#131/#144/#349 precedent — test-only change). |

| [#401](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/401) | ✅ Done | Warn on unusual weight/body-composition entries compared to the previous day | Reported live again with a screenshot — no warning shown even for clearly unrealistic body-composition values (muscle mass 7888kg, water 478%, bone mass 233kg, body fat 329%). Root cause: `saveBodyComposition()` never actually ran any schema validation for these 5 fields before persisting — `dailyEntryFormSchema.ts`'s existing `.max()` bounds (from #233) were defined but never enforced via a `safeParse` call the way weight's own save handler already did. Fixed by adding that missing `safeParse` step (mirroring `saveWeight()`/`saveBodyMeasurements()`) plus new absolute-plausibility soft-warning checks (`isUnusualMuscleMassKg`/`isUnusualVisceralFat`/`isUnusualBodyWaterPercent`/`isUnusualBoneMassKg`/`isUnusualBodyFatPercent` in `unusualEntryThresholds.ts`) alongside the existing delta-vs-previous-day checks — both now use the same "Save anyway/Fix it" pattern weight already has. **Follow-up**: `dailyEntryFormSchema.ts`'s hard `.max()` bounds were then widened/narrowed to match these new absolute-plausibility constants exactly (muscle 150→100, visceral 60→20, water 80→100, bone 20→30, body fat 80→100) — for these 5 fields there's no known legitimate real value above the plausible-max (unlike weight's rare-but-real outliers up to 400kg), so exceeding it is now always a hard block with no override, not a gap left for the softer warning to fill. New tests: `unusualEntryThresholds.test.ts` (24 tests) covers all 5 absolute-bounds functions, including the exact reported typo values. `tsc -b`/lint/targeted files green. Confirmed on-device. |

| [#410](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/410) | ✅ Done | Change night-eating cutoff from 9pm to 11pm | `NIGHT_EATING_CUTOFF_HHMM` in `nightEating.ts`: `'21:00'` → `'23:00'`. Every existing test fixture at the boundary already used `'23:00'` exactly, so only `nightEating.test.ts`'s own boundary cases needed updating (new `'22:59'` just-before-cutoff false case added). `tsc -b`/lint/6 directly affected test files (216 tests) green. |
| [#411](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/411) | ✅ Done | Import period (menstrual flow) data from Apple Health; researched other candidate fields | Reported live after re-importing (2 screenshots): the user's own Apple Health Cycle Tracking shows real period days (e.g. May 19-23, June 14-18, July 11-12), but History's calendar view shows no distinct period marker on any of those dates post-import. Original implementation notes above. Root cause found by inspecting the scanner's own regex rather than the `MenstrualFlow` mapping logic (which tested correctly in isolation the whole time): `RECORD_TAG_RE` only ever matched self-closing `<Record .../>` tags, but Apple Health writes the *paired* `<Record ...>...</Record>` shape whenever a record has `<MetadataEntry>` children — routine for manually-logged flow records (`HKMetadataKeyMenstrualCycleStart`). Every metadata-bearing record was silently dropped before ever reaching the patch builder — a general parser gap, not period-specific, that happened to surface there first since manual period entries usually carry metadata while sensor-logged samples (weight, steps) often don't. Fixed by widening the regex to match both shapes (self-closing, or a lazy match to the first `</Record>` — `Record` elements don't nest, so this stays complete/correct). New tests: the metadata-child shape (single/multiple entries, split across a chunk boundary, no false nesting into the next record), a perf-regression guard (the new lazy alternative doesn't reintroduce the buffer's already-once-fixed O(n²) trap), and an end-to-end scanner+builder test reproducing the exact scenario. `tsc -b`/lint/`appleHealthParser.test.ts` (31 tests) green. Couldn't verify against the user's actual real export file (don't have access to it) — asking for on-device re-import confirmation rather than closing on the synthetic reproduction alone. Confirmed on-device: re-exported and re-imported, real period days now show up correctly. |
| [#412](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/412) | ✅ Done | Deep sleep shows as missing (—) despite total sleep being correct, e.g. July 2 | Earlier midnight-merge fix was correct for pre-midnight segments, but the Jul 2 re-report was a different root cause. Real export shows **two sources**: AutoSleep wins dominant total (7h19m `AsleepUnspecified`, no `AsleepDeep` in HealthKit — its UI "Deep 1:15" never exports as staged deep) while Apple Watch has real `AsleepDeep` (~50m) with a shorter total. Deep was read only from the dominant source → `—`. Fix: keep dominant-source `sleepHours`, take `deepSleepHours` as max deep across sources for that night (not a sum). Analysis dump: `docs/turtle-steps-ideas/july-2-2026-sleep-analysis.md` (outside repo). `tsc -b`/lint/`appleHealthParser.test.ts` (33 tests) green. **Confirmed on-device**: after re-import, deep sleep displays (Watch `AsleepDeep`, not AutoSleep’s 1:15 — accepted; AutoSleep deep isn’t in HealthKit). |

New test confirms the gendered header. `tsc -b`/lint/4 affected test files (38 tests) green. |

Required a further split beyond #416: `DailyEntryFormMorning.tsx` (new) needs to render in a completely different part of `TodayScreen.tsx`'s tree from the rest of the form (past BMI/deltas/the reorderable stat-card group), so both share one live `useDailyEntryFormState` instance via a new `DailyEntryFormStateContext`/`useDailyEntryFormStateContext` (`DailyEntryFormTop.tsx` narrowed to Meals+Water only; `DailyEntryFormBottom.tsx`/`DailyEntryForm.tsx` switched from prop-passing to context). The whole block gates on `entryStatus` (not just the Morning slot) — `useDailyEntryFormState`'s `initialValues`/`entryIdentity` memoize once on mount, so the provider must not mount before `entry` is the real loaded value; caught by 2 failing `TodayScreen.test.tsx` tests before being fixed. `tsc -b`/lint/daily-log suite (380 tests)/history suite (90 tests)/full suite green. |
| [#420](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/420) | ✅ Done | Today date-navigator row: mismatched button/input heights (adopt same-row-same-height as a convention) | First fix (jump-to-today button given an explicit `h-12`) only addressed that one button. Reported live again: the Date `Input` itself now renders *taller* than the (correctly 48px) arrows/today button — likely the same class of native-`<input type="date">`-doesn't-fully-respect-explicit-height WebKit quirk `ARCHITECTURE.md` already documents for #139, this time in the opposite direction. Second fix attempt (244487e, `max-h-12`/`line-height`) measured via Playwright `boundingBox()` in both Chromium and WebKit (390×844 viewport) — all 4 elements render at exactly 48px in both engines, no mismatch reproducible this way. Per this repo's own precedent, Playwright's bundled WebKit (esp. on Windows) is a known imperfect stand-in for real iOS Safari on native form-control quirks specifically — not claiming fixed, asked the user to check the actual behavior on their iPhone before guessing a third CSS change blind. **Confirmed still broken on-device** (screenshot) — the Playwright measurement was indeed a false negative. User's suggestion for the next attempt: hardcode the Date input's height explicitly rather than relying on `max-h-12`/line-height alone. Third fix attempt: replaced the Tailwind `h-12 max-h-12` classes with an explicit inline `style={{ height: '3rem', maxHeight: '3rem', ... }}` plus `overflow-hidden` on the input — `overflow-hidden` is the actually-new piece here, forcibly clipping the box to exactly 48px regardless of what the native `<input type="date">` control's own internal chrome wants to render at, rather than just requesting 48px and hoping the browser honors it (which is what the previous `max-h-12` attempt already did, and apparently didn't reliably get respected on real iOS Safari). `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests) green. Given this exact bug's own history of Playwright false negatives, not re-verifying via Playwright and not closing — asking the user to check on their actual device before this is considered fixed. **Confirmed still broken on-device a third time** — the date input is still taller than its row siblings even with `overflow-hidden`. Not yet re-investigated (three blind CSS attempts in a row is enough — next attempt should get real measurements from the actual device first, e.g. a temporary debug readout, rather than a fourth guess). **Debug instrument added** (`?debug=420` on the Today screen): strips the Date input's height cap entirely so it renders at its real unconstrained height, and shows all 4 row elements' actual rendered heights as on-screen text — no devtools needed on-device. Per `button.tsx`'s own `icon-xl` comment, #139 hit this identical native-date-input-height WebKit quirk before, and the fix that worked there was the reverse of what's been tried here 3 times: stop shrinking the date input, measure its real on-device height, and grow the row's other elements to match — but that's only actionable once today's real number (which may have drifted from #139's original 48px measurement) is known. **On-device debug measurement** showed the native Date input's true unconstrained height (42px) is actually *shorter* than the 48px arrows/today button, not taller — yet the still-live 3rd attempt (`overflow-hidden` directly on the `<input>`) visually renders taller live regardless, confirmed again with a fresh on-device screenshot. Box-computes-right + overflow-hidden-already-present + still-visually-taller-only-on-real-iOS points at Mobile Safari's native form-control chrome not reliably respecting `overflow: hidden` set on itself. **Fourth fix attempt**: moved the fixed height + `overflow-hidden` off the `<input>` and onto a plain wrapping `<div>` instead, which has no such native-widget quirk — clips whatever the input paints internally rather than relying on the input to clip itself. `?debug=420` still available (now targets the wrapper) if this needs revisiting. `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests) green. Not closing — awaiting on-device confirmation given this bug's history of false negatives from non-device testing. **Reported live**: the 4th attempt looked "very bad" — the Date input's own right/bottom border was now visibly cut off. The clip did take effect this time (unlike attempt 3), but what it clipped was the input's own real rendered border, not invisible overflow — confirming the native control genuinely paints larger than 48px in both dimensions, so no box that size can contain it without cutting into its own chrome. **Fifth fix attempt**, per the user's own suggestion: stop constraining the input entirely and instead shrink the arrows/today button to match its real natural size (42px, measured uncapped on-device) — the same "match the native control instead of fighting it" principle #139 already established, just in the opposite direction since this control's natural size is smaller, not larger, than the row's old 48px. Scoped to just this row's `Button` overrides (`size-[2.625rem]`/`h-[2.625rem]`), not the shared `icon-xl`/48px class other Today rows (Weight/Sleep/Steps/Notes) still use. Updated the #420 test's expected height class (`h-12` → `h-[2.625rem]`) to match. `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests) green. **Confirmed fixed on-device.** |
| [#421](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/421) | ✅ Done | Stats accordion border reads as detached from the trigger (#418 follow-up) | One shared `rounded-lg border border-border p-3` wraps both the trigger and `CollapsibleContent` now (same treatment `DailyEntryFormMorning`/`DailyEntryFormBottom` already use), instead of the trigger having its own separate border box. Pure JSX wrap, no logic change. `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (85 tests) green. |
| [#422](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/422) | ✅ Done | Add a tooltip to the date-navigator checkmark explaining what it means (#405 follow-up) | Reported live right after validating #405 — the checkmark only carries a screen-reader-only `aria-label`, nothing visible for a sighted user. Implemented as proposed: the checkmark badge is now an `InfoTooltip` trigger, with a new `icon?: ReactNode` prop (defaults to the existing `Info` glyph) so the checkmark itself renders as the tappable icon instead of being replaced by a generic "i". Sibling of the `<Label>`, not nested inside it — a `<label>` implicitly extends its accessible name to any interactive element nested inside it, which would have made the button's name read as "Date This day has logged entries" instead of just the latter. New test confirms the tooltip text appears on click. `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (86 tests) green. |
| [#423](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/423) | ✅ Done | Today's night-eating toggle still has no reliable way to unselect (#406/#413 related) | First attempt added an explicit "×" Clear button (matching #413's History treatment) — reported live as still broken (clicking Clear made it default to "Yes"), reproduced even in a plain desktop browser with zero meals logged, ruling out any device/data-specific explanation. Root-caused via live Playwright against the real dev server (jsdom's own render of the exact same interaction passed cleanly throughout — a real false-negative from the test suite): Radix's `ToggleGroup`'s `useControllableState` treats a controlled `value` of `undefined` as "stop controlling me, keep your last internal state," not "explicitly show nothing selected," so the rendered `aria-checked` silently never cleared regardless of what `nightEatingOverride`/`nightEatingEffective` actually computed to. Fixed by passing `value={''}` instead of `value={undefined}` for the "nothing selected" case — a real, defined value Radix accepts as "no item matches," which keeps it in controlled mode. With this fixed, the ToggleGroup's own built-in tap-to-deselect (#406) works reliably on its own, so the Clear button added first is no longer needed and was removed. `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (105 tests) green; fix verified live via Playwright (not just jsdom, which didn't reproduce the bug). **Reopened a second time** — still reported broken live on real Safari and the installed PWA. Built a live cross-engine Playwright harness (Chromium + WebKit, seeded real IndexedDB, reading the actual persisted record after each click) covering both a day with a genuinely late meal logged and a completely blank day with zero food logged at all — the toggle's own tap-to-deselect worked correctly in every scenario tried, on both engines. Restored the explicit "×" Clear button (`state.nightEatingOverride`, re-exposed from `useDailyEntryFormState`) next to the ToggleGroup as a second, independent path to "no override." **Reopened a third time**, with screenshots: on a real day with a dinner logged at 19:0x (well before the 23:00 cutoff), tapping Clear switched the toggle to show "Нет" (No) pressed instead of nothing — the actual bug, finally pinned down from the screenshots: the toggle's displayed value was driven by `nightEatingEffective` (override *or else* the meal-derived fallback), so clearing an override on a day with real logged meals just revealed the derived answer, visually indistinguishable from a real pick. Pure app logic, not browser-specific at all — the earlier "Safari/PWA-only" framing was wrong, just a mismatch between the blank test data used while investigating and the user's real data. Fixed per an explicit 3-state spec from the user (Yes/No/nothing, Clear always produces "nothing" regardless of any meal data): the `ToggleGroup`'s `value` now reads from `nightEatingOverride` directly, never `nightEatingEffective`; the derived value moved to a new small caption (`t.dailyEntry.nightEatingAutoDetectedLabel`) shown only when there's no override, so it's still visible without being conflated with the toggle's own pressed state. New tests: derived value renders as caption text with neither button pressed; Clear resets to nothing selected even with a real logged meal that derives a definite answer (the exact reported scenario). Verified live via Playwright screenshots (seeded real IndexedDB, before/after clicking Clear). `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (110 tests) green. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#424](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/424) | ✅ Done | No way to leave a field's edit mode without saving (only a Save checkmark) | Reported live from Body composition's edit mode. Scope resolved via a comment: apply to all 5 pencil-to-edit fields (Weight, Sleep, Body measurements, Steps, Body composition), not just Body composition. Mechanism mirrors `MealList.tsx`'s existing Cancel button (#169) — ghost X button, exits edit mode without persisting — rather than inventing a new pattern; sized `icon-xl` (48px) to match each field's own row height, not `icon-sm` copied literally (MealList's own row is a different, shorter height). Each field gets `cancelEditX()` in `useDailyEntryFormState.ts`: reverts to the value from when this render's edit session started (`initialValues`, memoized once on mount — same accepted limitation already documented elsewhere in this hook), clears validation errors, dismisses Body composition's pending unusual-value warning too, exits edit mode. Cancel only renders when there's an established value to actually revert to (`canCancelXEdit`) — a brand-new field auto-opens in edit mode with nothing saved, and offering Cancel there would flip to a broken display render (its JSX assumes a real value); `alwaysEditable` mode (History's inline edit) always permits it, since Cancel there just clears the input safely. Body composition's grid (#427) gained a 4th column for Cancel at Save's own row rather than restructuring — verified via Playwright `boundingBox()` that Save's already-validated position is unchanged and Cancel sits directly beside it (same row, 24px gap), not guessed blind given #427's own 5-iteration history. New tests across all 5 fields (no Cancel with nothing saved yet, discard reverts to the saved value, Cancel also dismisses a pending #401 warning for Body composition). `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (122 tests)/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests)/History suite (90 tests) green. Confirmed on-device. **Found live during confirmation**: two more fields have the identical gap — the day note (`DailyEntryFormBottom.tsx`) and custom-metric per-day notes (`MetricValueRow`, #364) — but each uses its own separate edit-mode mechanism, not the 5-field `cancelEditX()` group this issue built, so filed separately as #437 rather than folded in. |
| [#437](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/437) | ✅ Done | Day note and custom-metric notes still have no way to leave edit mode without saving (#424 follow-up) | Spotted live in two screenshots while confirming #424 — the day note ("Заметка дня") and a custom metric's per-day note (e.g. "Акне") each still only show a checkmark Save button, no Cancel. Not covered by #424 because both use their own separate edit-mode implementation (`DailyEntryFormBottom.tsx`'s `Textarea`, #417; `MetricValueRow`'s read/edit toggle, #364) rather than the 5-field `cancelEditX()`/`canCancelXEdit` mechanism #424 built. Fixed both independently, same shape as #424's own buttons (ghost `X`, `icon-xl`/`icon-lg` to match each field's own button size, gated on there being an already-saved value to revert to): day note gets a new `cancelEditNote()` in `useDailyEntryFormState.ts` (reverts to `initialValues.note`, same `initialValues`-memoized-once-on-mount pattern #424's other fields use) + `canCancelNoteEdit`; the custom-metric note (a plain local-`useState` component, not react-hook-form) gets its own local `cancelEditNote()` reverting `noteDraft` back to the `note` prop. New Dictionary key `cancelEditNoteLabel` added to both `dailyEntry` and `customMetrics` sections (en/ru) — two separate keys since they're two independent features, not a shared one. New tests: no Cancel button with nothing saved yet, Cancel discards a typed change and reverts to the saved note (both fields). `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`/`CustomMetricLogSection.test.tsx` (132 tests combined) green. |
| [#438](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/438) | ✅ Done | Cancel (×) button in Morning entries edit mode does nothing when clicked (History) | Reported live with a screenshot — on History's `alwaysEditable` inline-edit view (Weight/Sleep/Body composition all open in edit mode at once), clicking the × Cancel button next to any of them does nothing; edit mode doesn't close, nothing reverts. Very likely a regression in #424's own new `cancelEditX()` buttons, surfaced only now since #424 was mainly exercised against Today's pencil-to-edit flow rather than History's always-editable rendering, but not yet confirmed. **Refined live**: reproduces only when editing a *previous* entry via History, not Today's own pencil-to-edit flow. Also a layout regression in this same context — Body composition's Save/Cancel buttons no longer sit adjacent in their #427-established grid cell, rendering far apart instead (screenshot). Investigated live via Playwright against a seeded previous-day entry in History — Cancel correctly reverted a typed muscle-mass change back to its saved value, no reproduction found this way. Confirmed working on-device — closing without a code change; the original report may have been a one-off (stale render, mis-click, or a state already fixed incidentally by #435/#436/#437's own changes to the same hook). |
| [#425](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/425) | ✅ Done | Move weeks-target-met list below Calendar/History view; add scroll or pagination | `MetTargetList` moved to render after the Calendar/List view section in `HistoryScreen.tsx` (was before the view-mode toggle). `MetTargetList.tsx`'s own list gained `max-h-96 overflow-y-auto`, same treatment `WeeklySummaryCards`/`MonthlySummaryCards`/outlier chip lists already use. Pure reorder + one class addition, no test needed (same precedent as #415/#416). `tsc -b`/lint/History suite (90 tests) green. |
| [#426](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/426) | ✅ Done | "Weeks target met" retroactively applies today's goal to weeks before it existed | First fix gated `targetMet` on the active goal's own `createdAt` — **reopened with screenshots**: the whole "weeks target met" list went completely empty even though real met weeks existed (confirmed via the Goal page's own Past goals list). Root cause: this app creates a fresh `Goal` record roughly every week ("Начать новую цель"/Start new goal), so the active goal's `createdAt` is almost always very recent — using it directly as the cutoff wiped out every earlier week the moment a new weekly goal started. Real fix: new `earliestGoalCreatedAt(goals)` in `goalHistory.ts` finds the one-time moment goal-tracking itself began, across *every* goal ever saved — not whichever one is currently active. `weeklySummaries()` takes this as a new, separate `goalTrackingStartDate` parameter (deliberately decoupled from the `goal` object used for `targetWeeklyLossKg`); no gating at all when a caller doesn't pass it (existing callers/tests unaffected). Threaded through `useHistoryData.ts`/`useDashboardData.ts` (both now fetch *every* goal — `useHistoryData.ts` already did for `reachedGoalWindows`) into `MetTargetList.tsx`/`WeeklySummaryCards.tsx`. New tests: the exact reported regression (multiple sequential weekly goals, a week after the earliest one but before the active one still evaluates), plus the original "predates goal-tracking entirely" and "same calendar day" cases, plus 3 new `earliestGoalCreatedAt` unit tests. Verified live via Playwright (seeded 3 sequential weekly goals + an active one created yesterday — real met weeks correctly show up again), not just unit tests, given the first attempt's own unit tests all passed while the real behavior was still broken. `tsc -b`/lint/`weeklySummaries.test.ts`+`goalHistory.test.ts`+`MetTargetList.test.tsx`+`WeeklySummaryCards.test.tsx`+`HistoryScreen.test.tsx`+`DashboardScreen.test.tsx` (72 tests) green. **Closed once already this session** on the strength of the first (incomplete) fix — reopened by the user, corrected, reopened again for this fix's own on-device confirmation. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#427](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/427) | ✅ Done | Body composition's Save checkmark ends up stranded below the field grid | Reported live with a screenshot. 5 sub-fields in a `flex flex-wrap` row alongside the Save button wrap unevenly (2 then 3), leaving the button with nowhere to fit and landing on its own row. Sleep (4 fields)/Body measurements (2 fields) don't have this problem. Direction resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: consistent grid over icon-only labels. Five iterations, each caught live with a screenshot: (1) plain `grid grid-cols-2` split the card's full width into two equal columns far wider than each narrow field, stranding the button in the empty right-hand space; (2) `w-fit` fixed the column sizing but moved the button below the grid on its own row — "It should not be below the input fields. It should be on the right"; (3) the button became the grid's 6th cell next to the last field, but only aligned with that one row; (4) centered the button against all 3 rows as a sibling column — "Can you align it with the second row vertically," confirming centering-across-3-rows happens to land near row 2 but isn't the same as being aligned *to* it; (5) explicit `grid-cols-[auto_auto_auto]`/`row-start-*`/`col-start-*` placement puts the button at column 3, row 2 specifically, `self-end` bottom-aligning it within that row — since the button (`size-12`) and each input (`h-12`) are both exactly 48px, this makes its top border land flush with the row's own input top border, not just visually close. Confirmed via a Playwright measurement (`boundingBox()` on both elements) showing an exact 0px difference, not just an eyeballed screenshot. Confirmed on-device; added a regression test afterward asserting the button carries the exact `col-start-3`/`row-start-2`/`self-end` classes the placement depends on (jsdom can't verify real position, but this guards against a future refactor silently reverting to grid auto-flow). `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests)/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (108 tests) green. |

Removed the button, `clearNightEatingOverrideLabel` (Dictionary/en/ru). Confirmed on-device; added a regression test afterward asserting the button never renders even once an explicit override exists (the prior removed test only covered the no-override case). `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (108 tests) green. |

Confirmed on-device; added a regression test afterward asserting the caption never renders even with a derived value from a logged meal. `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (108 tests) green. |
| [#430](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/430) | ✅ Done | Today's water-tracking card requires scrolling to reach add/remove controls | Tapping the "Remaining water" `StatCard` now smoothly scrolls to `#water-entry-section` in `DailyEntryFormTop`. `StatCard` gained an optional `onClick` (+ `cursor-pointer` when set). Found and fixed a real bug while adding tests: the card's eye-icon visibility-toggle action (rendered inside the same clickable div) didn't stop event propagation, so hiding the section also fired the scroll — added `stopPropagation()` in `VisibilityToggleButton`. Confirmed on-device; new tests cover the scroll firing on card click, the toggle click *not* triggering it, and `StatCard`'s own onClick/cursor-pointer behavior. `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests)/`stat-card.test.tsx` (10 tests) green. |
| [#435](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/435) | ✅ Done | Validate body-composition fields on blur, not only when Save is clicked | Surfaced directly while confirming #401's fix live — the 5 body-composition fields only validate at Save time, so an absurd value sits accepted-looking in the input until Save is clicked. Resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: on blur (not every keystroke, to avoid flashing an error on a half-typed number), scoped to just these 5 fields for now (not weight/waist/hip/sleep/steps, which stay Save-time-only). New `validateBodyCompositionFieldOnBlur()` reuses the exact same schema `saveBodyComposition()` already checks — doesn't replace that check, adds an earlier one. `DailyEntryFormMorning.tsx`'s new `bodyCompositionFieldProps()` composes it with react-hook-form's own `onBlur` (needed for its internal touched/dirty tracking) instead of duplicating the wiring 5 times. New tests: error shows on blur before Save is clicked, clears once fixed, doesn't flash while a value is only half-typed. Verified live via Playwright (no error while typing, error appears immediately on blur). `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (111 tests) green. Confirmed on-device. **Found live during confirmation**: the on-blur error text itself ("Invalid input") renders in raw untranslated English on an otherwise-Russian UI — filed separately as #436, since it's a pre-existing gap in every field validator here (#401 and earlier), not something #435 introduced. |
| [#436](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/436) | ✅ Done | Validation error messages show raw untranslated English text instead of localized copy | Spotted live in a screenshot while confirming #435 — body composition's muscle-mass field showed "Invalid input" in English despite the rest of the UI being in Russian. Root cause visible directly in `useDailyEntryFormState.ts`: every field validator (`saveWeight`/`saveNote`/`saveSleep`/`saveSteps`/`saveBodyMeasurements`/`saveBodyComposition`/`validateBodyCompositionFieldOnBlur`) did `setError(field, { message: result.error.issues[0].message })` — zod's own raw default English text, never routed through `Dictionary`/`t()` like every other user-facing string. Not new with #435 — the same pattern already existed in `saveBodyComposition()`/`saveWeight()` (#401 and earlier); #435 just surfaced it earlier (on blur) and made it visible live. Resolved directly (user's own choice among 3 options): translate zod's generic wording only, not per-field bounds copy mentioning each field's exact numeric limits — fewer strings to write/maintain, at the cost of not telling the user exactly what range is allowed. New shared `t.dailyEntry.invalidValueMessage` ("Invalid value."/"Неверное значение.") replaces all 13 raw-zod-message call sites in this one hook. Existing tests asserting on the old raw English text (`/Too small/`/`/Too big/` regexes, `DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`) updated to assert `/Invalid value/` instead — these were themselves testing the reported bug's own text. `SettingsScreen.test.tsx`'s identical-looking `/Too big/` assertion is unrelated (Settings' Profile height field uses a completely different schema/hook, `profileFormSchema.ts`, out of this issue's scope) and was left untouched. `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`/`TodayScreen.test.tsx`/`DayDetail.test.tsx` (240 tests combined) green. |

Kept the self-service steps as the primary path but added a note that a free-tier user can instead email MyFitnessPal support directly to request their data (`src/i18n/en.ts`/`ru.ts`'s `myFitnessPalImport.howToExportSteps`) — deliberately not framed as formal/legal ("GDPR") language, just a plain fallback instruction. `tsc -b`/lint/`ExportSection.test.tsx` (19 tests) green. |
| [#440](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/440) | ✅ Done | Add Sleep as a toggleable series in the Compare Data chart | Requested live — "Compare your data" (`CustomChartView.tsx`) offers Weight/Calories/Protein/Fat/Carbs/Water/Steps/Body fat %/Fasting hours/Period/Constipation/Night eating/custom metrics, but not Sleep. Added `sleep` (`entry.sleepHours`) as a new `NumericSeriesKey` — a plain per-entry field, same `SERIES_EXTRACTORS` shape `steps`/`waist`/`hip`/`bodyFat` already use, not the special-cased cross-day shape `fastingHours` needs. Also updated (all mechanically, to keep the `NumericSeriesKey` union exhaustive): `exportBundleSchema.ts`'s `numericSeriesKeySchema` enum, `metricRefLabel.ts`'s `builtinMetricLabel` switch, `customChartSelectionStore.ts`'s `DEFAULT_CHART_TYPES`.
