Given its own dedicated `--chart-sleep` color token (`index.css`) rather than reusing a freed-up generic `--chart-4`/`--chart-5` slot — those two are the exact ones #347/#350 already found render solid black once switched to bar mode, and sleep gets the same per-series bar/dots toggle fastingHours/bodyFat have. New test confirms `sleep` extracts from `sleepHours` correctly. `tsc -b`/lint/`customChartSeries.test.ts` (16 tests)/`CustomChartView.test.tsx` (26 tests)/`exportBundleSchema` suite green. |
| [#441](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/441) | ✅ Done | Mark abnormal (outlier) points on the Weight trend graph | Reported live with a screenshot — an obvious single-point data-entry-error spike on `WeightTrendChart.tsx` (Dashboard) renders identically to every real point, no visual flag. This app already has deterministic Tukey's-fences outlier detection (`domain/stats/outlierDetection.ts`, #224) with a dimmed/highlighted rendering treatment, but per #224's own scope it was only ever wired into the 6 correlation views, never the 4 main trend charts. Scoped to Weight only, matching the issue's own filed scope (not extended to the other 3 trend charts). Reuses `outlierBounds()`/`isOutlier()` directly against the raw weight values (a single dimension, not the X/Y-pair shape `flagOutliers` needs for the correlation views) — computed once per render, checked in the `Line`'s own custom `dot` renderer alongside the existing current-value-marker logic: an outlier point always renders as a `var(--destructive)` circle (r=4) regardless of whether it's also the last-logged-value index; a non-outlier, non-last-value point still renders nothing, unchanged. `tsc -b`/lint/`WeightTrendChart.test.tsx` (10 tests) green — the flagged-color rendering itself isn't unit-testable (Recharts renders no SVG internals under jsdom, same limitation documented elsewhere in this file), so **verified live via a seeded Playwright script** instead (9 days of real IndexedDB data with one deliberate 74kg spike against a ~58-59kg baseline): confirmed exactly one `circle` with `fill="var(--destructive)"` rendered at the spike, screenshot attached to the closing comment. Confirmed on-device. **Follow-up filed as #448**: user wants the outlier-flagging threshold itself changed from Tukey's-fences to a flat ±2kg band. |
| [#442](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/442) | ✅ Done | Trend chart "Open this day" link should go to Today, not History (#389 follow-up) | Confirmed on-device by the user. Reported live with a screenshot — `WeightTrendChart.tsx`'s tap-a-point tooltip "Открыть этот день →" link still targeted `/history?date=X`. #389 already retargeted `OutlierPointsList.tsx`'s equivalent link from History to `/?date=X` for the exact same reason ("#41's own older chart-tap-to-navigate mechanism (`WeightTrendChart.tsx` etc.) still targets History unchanged, not part of this report") — this issue was that deferred scope. Confirmed via grep that all 4 main trend charts duplicate the identical `to={\`/history?date=${date}\`}` line separately (not a shared component) — retargeted all 4 (`WeightTrendChart.tsx`, `CalorieTrendChart.tsx`, `MacroTrendChart.tsx`, `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx`) to `/?date=X`. `viewDayLink`'s copy ("View this day"/"Открыть этот день") doesn't explicitly say "History" the way #389's old copy did, so it was left unchanged — still reads correctly for the new destination. `tsc -b`/lint/all 4 trend chart test files (31 tests) green — the `Link`'s destination itself isn't independently unit-testable (Recharts renders no SVG internals under jsdom, same limitation documented elsewhere in this file), so this relies on the mechanical correctness of the change plus the passing existing suites. |
| [#443](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/443) | ✅ Done | Add previous/next navigation arrows to page through Dashboard graph periods | Requested live with a screenshot — no way to step to the previous/next month (or week/year) on the Dashboard graphs; only the fixed period picker (All time/Week/Month/Year/Custom, #380/#396) exists. Resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: the period *type* (Week/Month/Year) stays the one shared, global `DashboardPeriodPicker` control, but *which* window of that type each of the 4 main trend charts (Weight/Calorie/Macro/Body composition) currently shows is each chart's own independent local state — arrows render in each chart's own footer (next to its existing "Tap a point for details" hint), not as one shared control, so paging one chart back doesn't move the others. All time/Custom get no arrows (`isPageableTrendChartPeriod`) — neither has a natural "next unit." New shared hook `useChartPeriodPager` (per-chart local `periodsBack` state, reset to 0 whenever the shared period type itself changes — done during render, comparing against a stored previous value, not in a `useEffect`, per React's own anti-cascading-render guidance) resolves each chart's own window by shifting the anchor date back by one window's worth of days (7/30/365) via the existing `resolveTrendChartPeriodRange`'s injectable `today` param, reusing all its existing 'all'/'custom' logic unchanged. `DashboardScreen.tsx` now passes the *full* `entries` (not `periodFilteredEntries`) plus `period`/`customStart`/`customEnd` to just these 4 charts — every other Dashboard section (`CustomChartView`, the 6 correlation views, etc.) is unaffected, still reading the pre-filtered set. New `period`/`customStart`/`customEnd` props all default to `'all'`/`''`/`''` so every pre-#443 caller/test behaves identically without passing them. Also fixed a dead-end paging could otherwise create: a chart that returns `null`/an empty-state message when it has zero points in the *current* window used to do so unconditionally — now only when paging is inactive; when a chart is genuinely paged into an empty window, it still renders its title + the pager controls so the user isn't stranded with no way back. Range label uses a fixed `dd.MM.yy` format (matches #444's own fix for the same narrow-space-overlap class of bug), not a locale-formatted one. New `useChartPeriodPager.test.ts` (8 tests) plus a new "prev/next period paging (#443)" describe block in `WeightTrendChart.test.tsx` (3 tests). `tsc -b`/lint/all 6 affected test files (65 tests total) green. Verified live via Playwright (seeded 3 distinct weeks of real IndexedDB weight data): clicking the Weight chart's own Previous-period arrow paged only that chart back a full week while Calorie/Protein-fat-carbs/Body composition's own pagers correctly stayed on the current week, confirming true per-chart independence; Next-period arrow correctly disabled only at the current window. |
| [#444](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/444) | ✅ Done | Compare Data chart: right Y-axis missing for some series combinations (e.g. Weight + Carbs) | Reported live with 2 screenshots — with Weight + Calories selected, the right Y-axis renders correctly; with Weight + Carbs selected (same period/data otherwise), the right Y-axis is completely missing. Root-caused live via a seeded Playwright script measuring each `YAxis` group's actual DOM position: the always-present hidden `normalized` axis is `orientation="right"`, and the dual-axis pair's own visible "right" key is *also* `orientation="right"` — two same-side axes confuse Recharts' `width="auto"` margin-auto-expansion measurement, the identical bug class #393 already diagnosed (there, for the single-axis case, fixed by flipping the hidden axis's orientation). Here, flipping orientation isn't an option (it's already needed on the right side for #393's own case) — instead switched the dual/single axis' own `width="auto"` to a fixed `width={40}`, the exact fix `BodyCompositionTrendChart.tsx`'s own dual axis already uses for this reason, sidestepping the auto-measurement conflict entirely rather than trying to resolve it. Before the fix, measured live: Weight+Calories' right axis position (x=360) sat right at the container's edge (barely, inconsistently clipped); Weight+Carbs' (x=377) sat 16px past it (fully invisible) — confirming it wasn't really "Carbs-specific," just however wide that series' own formatted tick labels happened to push the mismeasured axis. After the fix, both combinations measure identically (x=373, consistent regardless of which series is selected). `tsc -b`/lint/`CustomChartView.test.tsx` (26 tests) green — the axis rendering itself isn't unit-testable (Recharts renders no SVG internals under jsdom), verified instead via the live Playwright measurement described above. **Reopened**: reported live again with a screenshot (Weight+Sleep, no right-axis values visible at all) — this "fix" didn't hold. The earlier verification was flawed: it only checked the `<g>` group's own bounding-box x-position, never the actual rendered tick `<text>` content/visibility, and that text-selector query silently returned empty for every case tested (including the left axis, already known-good from the screenshot) — so the verification told me nothing real about whether tick numbers were actually rendering. **Re-investigated properly**: a corrected live Playwright script (checking actual tick `<text>` content this time, correctly scoped to the real Compare Data chart after an earlier scoping mistake nearly gave a false result) confirmed the deployed `width={40}` fix genuinely works across every scenario tried — real data on both series, one series with zero logged values in range (correctly shows no ticks — nothing to plot, not a bug), and identical values every day (Recharts pads the domain and still shows 5 ticks). Could not reproduce the missing-axis failure through any data shape. Given the fix is confirmed deployed (live since 10:36 UTC, well before the reopen was reported) and unreproducible through Chromium-based testing, this may be a WebKit/iOS-specific SVG rendering quirk — same category as #420's own difficulty. Asked the user for the exact period selected and whether a hard-refresh still shows it broken, rather than guessing another fix blind. **User reproduced it live in Chrome's own device emulator** (localhost, confirmed up-to-date checkout including the `width={40}` fix) and provided the actual missing piece: a DevTools screenshot showing the right axis's tick-label group had a real, non-zero bounding box (28.35×187.42 — text genuinely rendering) but positioned entirely outside the visible container. **Real root cause found**: the always-present hidden `normalized` `YAxis` (`orientation="right"`, `hide`) had no `width` set at all, defaulting to Recharts' own ~60px default — even though invisible, this still counted toward Recharts' right-side layout stacking (a *third* YAxis element, alongside the 2 real dual-axis ones), pushing the real visible "right" axis outward by the hidden one's own phantom width. My earlier `width={40}` fix only made the *visible* axes' own reported width fixed instead of auto — it never addressed this hidden third axis silently claiming space too. Fixed by giving the hidden `normalized` axis an explicit `width={0}`. Re-verified live via Playwright, this time checking the real axis line + tick text position against the SVG's own actual bounding box (not just consistency across combos, my earlier mistake): right axis line now at x=313, tick text spanning 321–349, comfortably within the container's 29–361 bounds, zero overflow. **#444 follow-up, reported live in the same pass**: with both axes now actually rendering, the X-axis date labels (previously `'PP'`, e.g. "8 июл. 2026 г.") overlapped in the now-narrower plot area — switched to a fixed `dd.MM.yy` format ("16.07.26"), short enough to fit and with no locale-dependent month name needing `date-fns`' locale option. `tsc -b`/lint/`CustomChartView.test.tsx` (26 tests) green. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#445](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/445) | ✅ Done | Compare Data chart's fasting-hours toggle label reuses the wrong dictionary key | Reported live with a screenshot — the fasting-hours toggle button in "Compare your data" reads lowercase "часов голодания" (correct only as a sentence-tail phrase) instead of a proper standalone label. Root cause: `CustomChartView.tsx`'s `fastingHours` series config reused `t.dashboard.fastingHoursLegend` directly, which is also used as-is by `FastingWindowCorrelationView.tsx`'s chart legend and `metricRefLabel.ts` — correct in those two contexts, wrong for a standalone toggle button. Fixed with a new dedicated key (`customChartFastingHoursLabel`, en "Fasting hours"/ru "Часы голодания"), same naming convention `customChartWeightLabel`/`customChartCaloriesLabel` already use in this file — `fastingHoursLegend` itself is untouched, still used by its other two call sites. `tsc -b`/lint/`CustomChartView.test.tsx` (26 tests) green. A first implementation attempt was started mid-way through an active issue-logging session and reverted per direct instruction before being finished; this is the actual fix. |
| [#446](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/446) | ✅ Done | Body composition edit-mode layout breaks when editing a previous day's entry (reproduces in Chrome emulator) | Reported live with a screenshot — Body composition's edit-mode grid layout (#427's established Save/Cancel placement) breaks when editing a *previous* day's entry, not just via History (#438) but also via Today's own `/?date=X` deep link to a past date. Confirmed reproducing even in Chrome's device emulator (390×844 viewport) — rules out any real-device/WebKit-only theory. Likely worth investigating together with #438 given the shared #427 grid mechanism, but filed as its own issue per direct request. **Investigated live**: seeded a previous day's full body-composition entry via Playwright, tested both reported contexts (Today's `/?date=X` pencil-to-edit flow, History's `alwaysEditable` inline view) — in both, Save/Cancel measured and screenshotted correctly, adjacent on the same row with the expected 24px gap, matching #427's layout exactly. Could not reproduce with clean test data in either context, same pattern as #438 (also unreproducible, also confirmed working on-device without a code change). Not closing without the user's own re-check — asked whether it's still reproducing, and if so whether it's specific to a large/multi-year real dataset rather than a fresh one. **User pushed back directly with 2 more live screenshots** — correctly rejecting the "cannot reproduce" conclusion — showing it reproduces with perfectly ordinary values (not invalid data), and critically, a brand-new empty entry with *no Cancel button at all* still shows row 1 misaligned ("Висцеральный жир" wrapping to 2 lines while "Мышечная масса" stays 1 line). This proved the #424 Cancel column wasn't the root cause at all, just a red herring that happened to correlate. **Real root cause**: none of the 5 field labels (`Мышечная масса`/`Висцеральный жир`/`Вода в организме`/`Костная масса`/`Процент жира`) have any height constraint — each label+input pair is its own independent `flex-col`, so when one label's Russian text wraps to 2 lines (their English equivalents are short enough never to, which is exactly why my first test with English locale couldn't reproduce this) while its row sibling doesn't, that column's own input gets pushed down relative to its neighbor, since nothing keeps the two flex-cols in a row aligned to each other. Confirmed live via a Russian-locale Playwright script measuring each input's own Y position: before the fix, row 1's two inputs sat at different Y coordinates whenever exactly one of the pair wrapped; row 2 (Water/Bone mass) similarly at risk. Fixed by giving all 5 label `<span>`s a shared `min-h-8` (32px, the exact height 2 wrapped lines take at this `text-xs` size) — reserves the same vertical space whether or not a given label actually needs to wrap, so every row's inputs now start at an identical Y regardless of which label(s) wrap. Re-verified live: all 5 inputs' Y positions now match within their own row (920/920, 1016/1016), confirmed both by measurement and screenshot. `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (124 tests)/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests) green. |
| [#447](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/447) | ✅ Done | Invalid body-composition values persist despite a disabled Save button (#435 regression?) | Reported live with 2 screenshots — all 5 Body composition fields filled with values far outside `dailyEntryFormSchema.ts`'s hard bounds (muscle 58888kg, visceral fat 258, water 254%, bone mass 250kg, body fat 147%); the Save checkmark is visibly disabled, yet after a refresh the invalid values show as saved anyway. User's theory: #435's on-blur validation (`validateBodyCompositionFieldOnBlur()`) may itself be triggering a real persist as a side effect, bypassing the disabled Save button's own `safeParse` gate entirely. **Real root cause, different from the user's own theory**: the Save checkmark was never actually programmatically `disabled` at all (it just looks muted due to its `outline` button variant) — #435's on-blur handler itself never calls `persist()` either. The actual bug: *every* field's save handler (`saveWeight`/`saveSleep`/`saveSteps`/etc., not just `saveBodyComposition()`) calls `persist(getValues())`, and `getValues()` (no argument) returns *every* registered field's current value at once — including whatever invalid, never-validated draft is still sitting in Body composition's own inputs at that moment. So typing garbage into Body composition, then saving a completely *different* field (Weight, Sleep, Steps, the day note, mood, etc.), silently smuggles the still-invalid Body composition draft through, since that field's own dedicated validation only ever runs inside `saveBodyComposition()` itself. Fixed by adding one shared `sanitizeForPersist()` step inside `persist()` itself (the one choke point every save handler already goes through) — any schema-backed field that currently fails its own zod schema falls back to `initialValues` (the last value this render session actually started with) instead of being written through as-is; the field genuinely being saved is unaffected, since it was already validated by its own handler before ever calling `persist()`. New test: types an out-of-range muscle mass value, never saves Body composition, saves Weight instead, asserts the persisted entry's `muscleMassKg` stays `undefined` rather than picking up the invalid draft — confirmed this test genuinely fails without the fix (verified by temporarily reverting it) and passes with it. `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (125 tests) green. |
| [#448](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/448) | ✅ Done | Change Weight graph's abnormal-value detection from Tukey's-fences to a flat +/-2kg day-over-day band | Follow-up confirmed live right after validating #441 — the Tukey's-fences (1.5×IQR) outlier flagging works, but the user wants a deterministic, simpler threshold instead. Resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: compare each point against its own *previous calendar day's* logged weight (day-over-day delta), not the 7-day rolling average or the visible range's overall median — same "previous day" concept #401's existing save-time `isUnusualWeightDeltaKg` warning already uses elsewhere, just a flat 2kg band here instead of that check's own "3kg or 5%, whichever is larger." Implemented in `WeightTrendChart.tsx`: replaced `outlierBounds()`/`isOutlier()` with a `Map<date, weightKg>` built from canonical (not display-converted) weights, flagging a date only when the *immediately preceding calendar day* (via `date-fns` `subDays`, exact date match, not just "the previous logged point") also has a logged weight and the absolute delta exceeds 2kg — a day right after a logging gap is never flagged, since there's no adjacent-day entry to compare against. Computed against canonical kg specifically (not the kg/lb-converted `weight` array used for display) so the 2kg threshold means the same thing regardless of the user's unit setting, same reasoning `isUnusualWeightDeltaKg` already established. `tsc -b`/lint/`WeightTrendChart.test.tsx` (10 tests) green — the flagged-color rendering itself isn't unit-testable (Recharts renders no SVG internals under jsdom) — verified live via a seeded Playwright script instead (5 points: a small day-to-day change correctly unflagged, a +2.5kg jump flagged, the following -2.4kg drop flagged, then a 2-day gap into a wildly different 90kg value correctly *not* flagged since there's no adjacent-day entry to compare it to). |
| [#449](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/449) | ✅ Done | Day note Textarea doesn't grow to fit content when editing a previous day | Reported live with a screenshot — editing a *previous* day's note, the auto-growing `Textarea` (#417) stays at single-line height and clips longer content ("Testing longer note of the..." cut off) instead of expanding downward, the way it does on Today. Not actually specific to "previous day" — root cause: `Textarea`'s own `ref` callback called `resize(el)` (measuring `scrollHeight`) *before* forwarding the ref to `react-hook-form`'s `register()`, which is what actually writes the field's initial value onto the uncontrolled DOM element (there's no React `value` prop doing it). So the very first resize always measured an empty textarea, regardless of how long the real loaded note was — it only grew correctly once the user's own typing triggered a later `onInput`-driven resize, by which point the *original* pre-existing content's height had already been missed. This affects any note that already has long content when the field mounts (most visible editing a previous day, since a brand-new day's note is empty at mount either way — nothing to miss). Fixed by swapping the order: forward the ref first, then resize. New `textarea.test.tsx` stubs `scrollHeight` (jsdom never computes real layout) to depend on the element's own value length, confirming resize picks up a value set by a simulated RHF-style ref — this test fails against the old ordering and passes against the fix. `tsc -b`/lint/new `textarea.test.tsx` (2 tests)/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (124 tests) green. |
| [#450](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/450) | ✅ Done | Fasting window toast doesn't recalculate when a previous day's last meal time changes retroactively | Reported live with exact repro steps — previous day's meal at 10pm, today's breakfast at 10am shows a correct 12h fasting toast; navigating back and logging one more meal at 4am on the previous day (now the real last meal) should recalculate today's toast to 6h, but it stays stuck at the stale 12h value. Root cause confirmed: `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal` (#287) only ever fires from *this* day's own first-timed-meal save — editing a different, earlier day never touches it. New `reconcileFastingWindowToastForPreviousDayEdit(nextEntries)`, called alongside it at the same 3 save sites: if the currently-shown toast's own date is exactly this day's next calendar day, re-fetches that day fresh from the repository and recomputes `fastingHoursBetween` using this save's `nextEntries` as the new previous-day side, updating (or dismissing) the toast. New test confirmed to genuinely fail without the fix (temporarily reverted to check) and pass with it — seeds both days, pre-sets a stale toast, edits the previous day, confirms the store updates, then remounts today's own `MealList` (matching the real navigate-back flow) and confirms the updated text renders. `tsc -b`/lint/`MealList.test.tsx` (61 tests) green. Confirmed on-device. **Note**: #456 later replaced this whole mechanism (the store, `reconcileFastingWindowToastForPreviousDayEdit` included) with a plain derived value — true for free, no reconcile call needed. |
| [#451](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/451) | ✅ Done | Move Compare Data chart's unit suffixes from Y-axis ticks to the series toggle buttons | Requested live with a screenshot (arrows at "Вес"/"Вода" toggle buttons and at "кг"/"мл" repeated on every Y-axis tick) — move each series' unit off the axis tick labels (bare numbers only) and onto its own toggle button instead (e.g. "Вес (кг)"). Scope resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: applied consistently to every series with a unit (Weight/Calories/Protein/Fat/Carbs/Water/Waist/Hip/Body fat/Fasting hours), not just Weight/Water — Steps/Sleep are unaffected, having never had a unit suffix to begin with. `useNumericSeriesConfig`'s return type gained `formatAxisTick` (bare number, used only by the 3 Y-axis `tickFormatter` call sites) and an optional `unit` field, alongside the existing `formatRaw` (kept as-is, still unit-suffixed, for the tooltip/legend's own per-point value text — deliberately not touched, since only the axis-tick repetition was reported as the problem). The series-selection `ToggleGroupItem`'s label now appends `` ` (${unit})` `` when a unit exists. Updated 11 existing `CustomChartView.test.tsx` assertions that queried buttons by their old, unit-less names. `tsc -b`/lint/`CustomChartView.test.tsx` (26 tests) green — verified live via Playwright that the Y-axis renders bare numbers (e.g. "79.2"/"80.0") while the toggle buttons read "Weight (kg)"/"Calories (kcal)"/etc. |
| [#452](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/452) | ✅ Done (closed as not a bug) | Weight chart's "7-day average" label questioned against the selected Chart period (Month) | Reported live with a screenshot, as a question rather than a confirmed bug — the dashed-line legend reads "среднее за 7 дней" while "Chart period" is set to Month; unsure if the label is accurate for this view or should read as a month-long average instead. **Investigated, confirmed not a bug**: `WeightTrendChart.tsx`'s `ROLLING_WINDOW_DAYS = 7` is hardcoded, entirely independent of the Chart period selector — the picker only widens/narrows the displayed date *range*, never the averaging window itself. Confirmed directly in `rollingAverage.ts`'s own sliding-window implementation: each plotted day sums only the trailing up-to-7 calendar days available to it, never 30 — so the label is accurate for Month same as any other period. Related minor wrinkle noted (not the reported question, not fixed): near the very start of any period/paged-back view, the first few days' own average can reflect fewer than 7 real days, since days just before the visible window aren't available to average over. Closed by direct request, tagged `invalid` (no closer stock label than "not a bug") — no code change, so no release note. |

## Tier 82 — Add-meal redesign + live feedback (2026-07-30)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#454](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/454) | ✅ Done | Redesign "Add a meal" from an inline accordion into a dedicated modal/flyout | User shared a reference mockup: the add-meal flow becomes a persistent full-screen flyout (`AddMealDialog.tsx`, new) instead of `MealList.tsx`'s old inline expand-in-place row, with the barcode scanner folded into the search input itself. Three forks resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: persistent multi-add (stays open across several single-dish adds, not one-shot-confirm), one barcode entry point (manual entry becomes a "Can't find it? Add manually" fallback link), and building the new meal-level "was it tasty?" reaction (`CalorieEntry.reaction?: Emotion`) as part of this issue rather than deferring it. `FoodPickerDialog.tsx` kept, not deleted — still used by an already-existing meal's own edit-mode "Find food," a different flow out of this issue's scope; its search/quantity logic is duplicated rather than shared into `AddMealDialog.tsx`, a deliberate tradeoff to avoid risking that still-heavily-tested flow. Found and fixed 2 real regressions surfaced only by rewriting `DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`'s old add-row coverage against the new flow (a sibling test file to `MealList.test.tsx`, exercising the identical `MealList` component one layer up, that the initial implementation pass missed entirely): the per-100g/per-portion mode toggle silently stopped converting already-typed values when switched (ported `MealList.tsx`'s own `updateEditItemMode` conversion math into `AddMealDialog.tsx`), and the FoodPickerDialog-era "today would be" live preview (kcal + macros, #278) was missing from the new confirm-quantity step entirely. Also restored two features the rewrite dropped for *new* meals specifically (both still worked fine when *editing* an already-saved meal, confirming they were rewrite gaps, not deliberate removals): the shared per-meal note field (confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` — restore, don't match the mockup's simpler look) and the app-level Clear-time button (#117 — the native time-picker Reset is unreliable, not a mockup-scope question). A second sweep of the full suite also caught one more stale reference in `DayDetail.test.tsx` (History's own mount of `MealList`, checking for the old "+ Add item" button name). Fully verified: `tsc -b`/lint/full suite (147 files, 1583 tests) green; live Playwright walkthrough against the dev server (persistent multi-add via search, a real barcode lookup against Open Food Facts, the reaction picker, Done, then History's expanded row rendering identically) all passed. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#455](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/455) | ✅ Done | Let user exclude abnormal-weight points from the Weight trend graph | Reported live with a screenshot of the Overview weight chart showing several red-flagged points (#441/#448's outlier detection) — wants the same kind of exclude-a-point mechanism the correlation charts already offer. Reused the correlation views' own shared bookkeeping (`useOutlierExclusionStore` — a plain per-view-key/per-date `excluded` map, persisted to localStorage) and their shared `OutlierPointsList.tsx` chip-list component directly, rather than the `useOutlierExclusion` *hook* — that hook's own flagging is 2D Tukey's-fences (`flagOutliers`, X/Y pairs), but `WeightTrendChart.tsx`'s #448 flagging is a flat day-over-day kg delta, a fundamentally different shape; only the store + list component generalize. One stable view key (`'weightTrend'`, only ever one Weight trend chart). An excluded point renders dimmed (`opacity: 0.3`) rather than removed from the chart — same visual treatment `outlierScatterShape.tsx` already gives an excluded correlation point — and deliberately does **not** cascade: it doesn't affect the rolling average, and doesn't stop a *neighboring* day's own delta from being flagged (the excluded point's real value still counts as "yesterday" for whoever comes after it) — kept to the literal ask ("similar to the correlation charts") rather than inventing new cross-point semantics. New tests cover the Exclude chip appearing for a flagged point, none appearing when nothing's flagged, and the Exclude/Restore toggle round-trip — the dot's own dimmed rendering isn't unit-testable (Recharts renders no SVG internals under jsdom, same limitation already documented for #441/#448), verified live instead via a seeded Playwright script (logged 3 real days through the actual UI, confirmed exactly one flagged circle at `opacity: 1`, clicked Exclude, confirmed it dropped to `opacity: 0.3` and the chip flipped to "Restore"). `tsc -b`/lint/`WeightTrendChart.test.tsx` (16 tests) green. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#456](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/456) | ✅ Done | Fasting-window notification should be persistent, not dismissible | Reported live — the #287 fasting-window toast can currently be dismissed via its own × close button. First pass removed just the manual dismiss button. **Reported still broken live**: "I still do not see the notification" — the *real* gap was deeper than the close button: the old toast was an action-triggered store (`announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal`, only ever fired at the moment a save made the condition newly true), not a persistent status display, so simply reopening the app later never re-showed it, independent of dismiss state at all. Confirmed via direct follow-up: "I want the information... to display constantly." Rebuilt as a plain derived value instead — `useMemo(() => previousDayEntry ? fastingHoursBetween(...) : null, [previousDayEntry, calorieEntries, dayStartTime])` in `MealList.tsx` — deliberately `useMemo` rather than a bare inline computation, per an explicit "always recomputed is performance heavy, compute on page load" constraint: it only recomputes when one of its 3 real inputs actually changes, not on unrelated re-renders. This let the entire old mechanism be deleted outright: `stores/fastingWindowToastStore.ts`, `announceFastingWindowIfFirstMeal`, `reconcileFastingWindowToastForPreviousDayEdit` (#450's own retroactive-recalc patch — now true for free, since `previousDayEntry` naturally reflects whatever's actually persisted), and #301's explicit dismiss-on-delete call (a day with no timed meal left naturally derives `null`). Rewrote the "recalculates retroactively" test to directly seed the previous day's *already-updated* record and confirm one fresh render reflects it correctly, and deleted one test ("does not re-show for a second meal") whose premise (first-meal-only firing) no longer applies — the toast now correctly shows consistently whenever the condition holds, not just once per triggering save. `tsc -b`/lint/`MealList.test.tsx`+`MealEditScreen.test.tsx`+`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (186 tests) green. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#457](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/457) | ✅ Done | Let portion-mode entry also record the portion's weight, to back-calculate per-100g macros | Reported live — when logging a food item in Portion mode (typing the total kcal/macros directly), there's currently no way to also enter the portion's weight in grams so the app could back-calculate a per-100g rate too (#121 made the equivalent field a read-only memory-aid badge in Portion mode). The domain math already fully supported this — `CalorieItem` only ever stores absolute totals + `amountG`, and `ratesFromAbsolute()` already back-calculates a per-100g rate from those on demand wherever one's needed (re-opening an item for edit, an autocomplete restore) — so this was purely a UI gap: `MealItemEditorSheet.tsx`'s Portion-mode field went from a real `NumberField` (bound to `amountG`/`onAmountGChange`, already threaded end-to-end) to a static "Portion" badge. Restored it as a real, optional field, now labeled `itemWeightLabel` ("Weight (g)") in Portion mode vs. `itemPortionsLabel` ("× 100g") in per-100g mode. **Real bug found and fixed along the way**: the field holds a genuinely different *unit* in each mode (a portions count in per-100g mode, e.g. "0.5" meaning 50g, vs. real grams in Portion mode) — `changeManualDraftMode()`/`updateEditItemMode()` (the mode-switch handlers in `AddMealDialog.tsx`/`MealList.tsx`) never converted between the two before, since the field was never visibly editable in Portion mode; converting it live exposed that the *existing* Portion→per-100g direction was already wrong even before this issue (it ran the value through `portionsToGrams()`, multiplying an already-real-grams number by 100 again) — confirmed via a regression test that fails without the fix (150g wrongly back-calculated as 3 kcal/100g via 15,000g instead of the correct 300 kcal/100g via 150g). Fixed by converting the field explicitly on every mode switch (new `gramsToPortions()` in `macroScaling.ts`, the inverse of the existing `portionsToGrams()`), moved outside the "nothing typed yet" early-return guard so setting the weight *before* the amount (a normal order) still converts correctly. Scoped to the daily-log entry point only (`MealItemEditorSheet.tsx`, shared by `AddMealDialog.tsx`'s manual entry and `MealList.tsx`'s edit-mode) — `RecipeEditorDialog.tsx` and `MealItemsSection.tsx` (Settings' food database editor) have their own independent copies of the identical per-100g/portion toggle UI with the same gap, left untouched as a natural, easy follow-up if wanted. `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`+`MealEditScreen.test.tsx`+`MealList.test.tsx`+`AddMealDialog.test.tsx` (205 tests) green. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#458](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/458) | ✅ Done | Muscle mass imported incorrectly, possibly confused with BMI | Reported live with 3 screenshots — Zepp Life shows "Muscle 37.63 kg" below "BMI 21.7"; this app's chart had a steady ~22-23kg then a jump to ~37-38kg; Today's summary for the same day showed 22.26kg. **Root cause**: not BMI confusion — Zepp's BODY CSV column is named `muscleRate` but the value is already kg (37.63 on 2026-07-27 matches the UI exactly). Pre-#458 `buildZeppLifePatches` wrongly did `weight * muscleRate / 100` → 59.15 × 37.63 / 100 = **22.26**, which is exactly the bad Today value and looks BMI-like by coincidence for this weight/height range. Fix: map `muscleRate` straight to `muscleMassKg`. Re-importing the Zepp export (imported value wins per field) overwrites the old wrong ~22kg values. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#459](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/459) | ✅ Done | Add-meal flyout doesn't match the proposed design; bring meal-editing onto it too (#454 follow-up) | Reported live comparing 3 fresh screenshots of the shipped `AddMealDialog` against the original #454 mockup: Recent should cap at 3 + "Show all", the quick-action row should be 3 prominent cards (not tiny text links), the reaction picker should be large labeled icons evenly spread (not small/bunched), spacing throughout should be more generous, and editing an already-saved meal (the pencil icon) should move onto the new flyout design too — a deliberate #454 scope boundary, now explicitly requested. Two forks resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: add a dedicated "Scan barcode" card back (in addition to the search bar's own icon, reversing #454's "one entry point" decision) to match the mockup exactly; and yes, migrate `MealEditScreen.tsx` onto `AddMealDialog` (with per-item edit-in-place and whole-meal delete added to close the resulting functionality gap). Implemented: Recent caps at 3 (`RECENT_COUNT`) with a "Show all"/"Show less" toggle (the collapse-back direction added after live feedback caught it missing); quick actions are now 3 bordered `QuickActionCard`s (Add food/Scan barcode/Log recipe) — the barcode card needed a disambiguating `aria-label` (`scanBarcodeButton — mealLabel`) since its visible text duplicates the search bar's own icon button's name; the "Was it tasty?" reaction picker got a new `EmotionPicker` `layout="spread"` variant (large `icon-xl` buttons, `justify-between`, a visible text label under each) plus a new `mealReactionValueLabel` translation (Yes/So-so/No) distinct from the day-mood `emotionLabel`; spacing bumped modestly throughout. Per-item edit-in-place: tapping a "meal so far" row (or its new pencil icon) opens the same manual-entry sheet pre-filled via a new `startEditItem()`, using `perPortion` mode as a direct value passthrough (no rate math needed) since `totalFromPortion` already passes typed values straight through; `saveManualDraft()` now branches on a new `editingItemId` state to call a new `onUpdateItem` prop instead of `onAppendItems`. Whole-meal delete: new optional `onDeleteMeal`/`mealPosition` props gate a header trash icon with the same 2-step `t.history.confirmDelete*` confirm pattern used elsewhere. `MealEditScreen.tsx` rewritten to render `AddMealDialog` directly (`open` always true, `onOpenChange(false)` → `navigate(-1)`) instead of `MealList` in single-meal-focus mode — its own repository-backed handlers wire `onAppendItems`/`onUpdateItem`/`onRemoveItem` (removing the last item deletes the whole meal, same invariant as `MealList.tsx`'s own `removeItemFromNewMeal`) /`onDeleteMeal` against the one `CalorieEntry`. Two scope decisions made explicitly via `AskUserQuestion` given the migration's size: (1) the old MealEditScreen UI supported simultaneous multi-item inline editing and meal-label renaming, neither of which `AddMealDialog` has (one-item-at-a-time via a sheet, no label field) — user chose to replace the old 787-line test suite with a lean new one for the new model rather than extend `AddMealDialog` to preserve every old capability; (2) the fasting-window toast (#287/#456) lives inside `MealList.tsx` itself, so it no longer shows on this dedicated edit screen (still fully works via Today/History/the normal add-meal flow) — user chose to skip restoring it here rather than add a second repository fetch + thread it into `AddMealDialog` as a new prop, deferred to **#460**. `tsc -b`/lint/`AddMealDialog.test.tsx`+`MealList.test.tsx`+`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`+new `MealEditScreen.test.tsx` green. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#460](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/460) | ❌ Won't fix | Fasting-window toast doesn't show on the dedicated meal-edit screen (#459 follow-up) | Deferred out of #459 (see above) rather than adding a second repository fetch for the previous day's entry plus a new `AddMealDialog` prop just for this one screen's edge case. Re-examined and closed rather than implemented: the toast was never a deliberate feature of the edit screen — pre-#459, `MealEditScreen` was a thin wrapper around the full `MealList`, which fetches `previousDayEntry`/`dayStartTime` internally and renders the toast unconditionally regardless of caller; the edit screen only ever had it as an inherited side effect of sharing `MealList`'s implementation. The edit screen exists to correct an already-logged meal, not to decide when/what to eat (the toast's actual purpose, already fully served on Today/History) — not worth a second fetch + a new `AddMealDialog` prop for that. |
| [#461](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/461) | ✅ Done | Blank screen after closing meal-edit flyout in PWA (post-#459 regression) | Reported live (and reopened the same day after the first CSS inset fix was confirmed): blank screen after closing the meal-edit flyout in the installed iOS PWA only — recovers on any tap. Earlier attempts (#1 inset fullscreen DialogContent from screen edges; #2/#3 move nested sheets off `fullscreen`) only sidestepped triggers; root cause was never identified. **4th fix**: the blank wasn't a compositing bug at all. After #459, editing a meal navigated to `/entry/:date/meal/:mealId` (`MealEditScreen`), which rendered *only* a portalled `AddMealDialog` — `<main>` was genuinely empty while the flyout was open, and closing called `navigate(-1)`, relying on standalone-iOS-PWA history to remount Today. Confirmed via devtools. Fix: removed the dedicated route + `MealEditScreen` entirely; the pencil now opens `AddMealDialog` as a state-controlled overlay inside `MealList` (`editingMealId`), same as the add-new-meal path — Today/History never unmount. Dead `focusMealId`/`focusMealPosition`/`onFocusedMealDone` props and the old inline-edit branch (unused since #459 already moved editing onto `AddMealDialog`) deleted with it. Also fixed `updateEditingMealNote` not trimming mid-keystroke (input reads straight back from `editingMeal.note`). Tests: lean overlay wiring in `MealList.test.tsx`; e2e no longer asserts the `/entry/...` URL; History pencil tests updated. Confirmed on-device — blank screen no longer reproduces. |
| [#453](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/453) | ✅ Done | Add previous/next navigation arrows to the Compare Data chart | Follow-up filed live right after confirming #443 on-device — the 4 main trend charts now page independently, but "Compare your data" (`CustomChartView.tsx`) still had none. Exactly the small wiring job anticipated: `CustomChartView` gained the same `period`/`customStart`/`customEnd` props the 4 trend charts already have (all defaulting to `'all'`/`''`/`''` so every pre-#453 caller/test is unaffected), calls `useChartPeriodPager` and aliases its `pagedEntries` to what was previously the raw `entries` prop, and renders `ChartPeriodPagerControls` at the end of its own card (no existing "Tap a point" footer hint to anchor next to, unlike the 4 trend charts, so it just sits as the card's own last child). `DashboardScreen.tsx` now passes this chart the full `entries` + period info too, same as the 4 trend charts. Also extended the same "don't strand the user in an empty paged window" fix #443 already applied to the other 4: the `entries.length === 0` branch only skips rendering entirely when paging is inactive. New "prev/next period paging (#453)" describe block in `CustomChartView.test.tsx` (2 tests). `tsc -b`/lint/`CustomChartView.test.tsx` (28 tests)/`DashboardScreen.test.tsx` green. Verified live via Playwright (seeded 2 distinct weeks): clicking only Compare Data's own Previous-period arrow paged its range back a week while the other 4 charts' own pagers stayed on the current week, confirming the same true per-chart independence #443 established. |

| [#462](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/462) | ✅ Done | КБЖУ summary row: add calories + a second row showing remaining macros | New `macrosSummaryTextWithCalories()` in `macroDisplay.ts` (kcal + 3 macros, null when all four unset) — used only by `useDailyEntryFormState.ts`'s day-level `dayMacrosSummary`/new `dayRemainingMacrosSummary`, not by `macrosSummaryText()`'s other callers (per-meal rows, weekly/monthly summaries) which already show calories separately. Remaining values (`remainingKcal`/`remainingProteinG`/`remainingFatG`/`remainingCarbG`) read 3 new `useGoalStore` selectors (`dailyProteinTargetG`/`dailyFatTargetG`/`dailyCarbTargetG`, mirroring the existing `dailyCalorieTargetKcal` one) minus consumed, same unclamped-overage shape TodayScreen's own remaining-nutrient cards already use (#266/#321) — negative reads as over budget, no separate framing needed for a plain text row. Generalized that exact substitution to every `text-xs text-muted-foreground` spot in `MealList.tsx`'s read-only meal card (the note, the meal-level macro total, each dish's own row), bumped the card's own padding `p-3`→`p-4` and gap `gap-1.5`→`gap-2`, and the meal header (name+kcal+time) `text-sm`→`text-lg font-medium` so it stays the most prominent line despite the now-larger body text. Two more live follow-ups in the same pass: split each dish's kcal/amount and macros onto two separate `<p>` lines (the combined one wrapped mid-sentence at the bigger font on a phone width) and added `divide-y divide-foreground/15` between dish `<li>`s so it's clear which macro line belongs to which dish (a first pass using `divide-border` was reported "barely visible" — that token is nearly the same tone as the card background in every theme; switched to the same `foreground/15`-style opacity token the card's own outer `ring-foreground/10` already uses, which does show up against `bg-card`). `tsc -b`/lint/`MealList.test.tsx`+`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` green. Confirmed on-device. |
| [#465](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/465) | ✅ Done | Today date-navigator row: Date input width changes depending on whether the jump-to-today button is present (#420 follow-up) | Reported live with 2 screenshots comparing the row with/without the "Сегодня" button visible — the Date input renders noticeably wider once that sibling button is absent (today's own date). Likely cause: the input is the row's only non-`shrink-0` element (`min-w-0`/`max-w-48`, no fixed width), so it absorbs whatever space a missing sibling frees up, capped at 192px (`max-w-48`). A local Playwright check at a matching 390×844 viewport did not reproduce the width difference (both scenarios measured 192px identically in desktop Chromium) — same category as #420's own Chromium-vs-real-Safari native-`<input type="date">`-rendering mismatch, so this needs a real on-device number rather than a desktop one. Extended the (renamed) debug instrument from `?debug=420` to `?debug=465`, now reporting width×height for all 4 row elements on-screen. `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests) green. **On-device measurement**: 179px with the today button present, 214px without — and 214 exceeds `max-w-48`'s own 192px cap, confirming that constraint wasn't actually binding on this native control at all, the same class of quirk as #420's height bug. **Fixed (1st attempt)**: wrapped the Date input in a plain `<div>` with a fixed pixel width (185px) + `overflow-hidden`, same wrapper-div shape as #420's own working fix, rather than trusting a width/max-width class on the input directly. `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests) green. **Reported live with 2 screenshots**: the Date input's right border was now visibly cut off — the exact same failure mode as #420's own 4th attempt: the native control paints wider than whatever box it's given, so clipping (however applied) cuts into its own real border again. **Fixed (2nd attempt), at the actual source**: the "Сегодня" button is now always rendered (previously conditionally mounted via `date !== todayIso()`), hidden via `invisible`/`aria-hidden`/`disabled`/`tabIndex={-1}` instead of unmounted when viewing today — this keeps the row's layout footprint, and therefore the Date input's available flex space, constant regardless of the viewed date, so the input's natural width never changes and needs no explicit constraint at all. Reverted the wrapper-div back to a plain `Input`. `aria-hidden` keeps the always-rendered-but-hidden button out of testing-library role queries (confirmed: the existing "has no Today button while already viewing today" test still passes). `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (88 tests) green. **Confirmed fixed on-device.** |
| [#466](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/466) | ✅ Done | Rename Today page/tab to a date-neutral name (Day) — fixes misleading today-copy while browsing history | Confirmed on-device by the user. Copy-only rename: `nav.today` / `today.title` → "Day"/"День"; `today.description` date-neutralized; swept Settings/Dashboard/History/custom-metrics i18n that named the screen. Routes/component names/`jumpToTodayButton` unchanged. Home icon left as-is (open question in the issue). |

Reported live with screenshot: the stat cards (Осталось воды, Сон) read as "very good by design, fonts, style, size and everything," but the КБЖУ/Осталось cards (#462) right below them look visually plainer by comparison — smaller label, no large bold number, no eye icon/progress-bar treatment. Requested: (1) restyle both cards to match StatCard's font/style/size, (2) wrap both inside a bordered accordion, similar to how the stat cards are grouped. Rebuilt both cards on the actual `StatCard` component (`src/shared/ui/stat-card.tsx`) instead of a plain `Card` — kcal as the big value/unit, the protein/fat/carbs breakdown as the description underneath, same shape TodayScreen's own "remaining" cards already use. Both wrapped in the same `rounded-lg border border-border p-3` + `Collapsible` accordion TodayScreen's Stats section uses (local `useState`, not persisted — narrower scope than Stats' own persisted collapse). `useDailyEntryFormState.ts` now also exports `remainingKcal`/`dayMacrosDescription`/`dayRemainingMacrosDescription` (already-computed local values, no new logic) so `DailyEntryFormTop.tsx` can feed `StatCard`'s value/description slots directly. The macros-only description is built directly off `t.dailyEntry.macrosSummary(...)` rather than reusing `macrosSummaryText()` — that helper returns `null` once all 3 macros are unset, correct for its other callers (whole row hidden then) but wrong here where the card can still be showing kcal alone, which would've silently dropped the "Protein — · Fat — · Carbs —" description line. 3 new i18n keys (`consumedMacrosLabel`, `expandMacrosLabel`, `collapseMacrosLabel`) in both locales. Updated 4 `DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` assertions whose target text moved from one combined string to a `StatCard`'s separate value/description nodes (scoped via `[data-slot="card"]`, same pattern `TodayScreen.test.tsx` already uses for its own StatCards). `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (127 tests) green. Confirmed on-device. |

Removed 4 now-obsolete drag-behavior tests from `DailyEntryForm.test.tsx` (plus its `@dnd-kit/core` mock scaffolding). Second live follow-up, screenshot comparison against the Remaining/Consumed StatCards: the meal note, meal-level macro line, and per-item detail block were still `text-base`/`text-xl` from #464's own "too condensed" fix — bigger than `StatCard`'s actual `text-sm` description size, so they read as mismatched once sitting directly below cards using it. Brought all three down to `text-sm` to match exactly; the meal header (name — kcal · time) untouched. `tsc -b`/lint/`DailyEntryForm.test.tsx`+`MealList.test.tsx`+`MealEditScreen.test.tsx`+`DayDetail.test.tsx`+`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (283 tests, then 192 re-run after the font fix) green. |

| [#469](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/469) | ✅ Done | Weekly goal card doesn't state which weight the -X kg target is relative to | Confirmed on-device by the user. Reported live with screenshot (weekly goal card circled): "Цель на эту неделю" shows "-0,1 кг похудения" without saying which weight it's computed from. Two placement suggestions offered in the report: bracket the reference weight next to the value, or reframe as an absolute target weight for the week alongside the delta. Investigated: the figure (`weeklyPace`/`goal.targetWeeklyLossKg`) is a flat weekly-pace target, not computed from any specific weight at all — the ambiguity is real, there's no baseline hiding in the calculation to surface. Fixed by appending a reference weight to the card's existing description line, alongside the week's date range (`· from 59.6 kg`) — omitted entirely when no weight has ever been logged, same as the description's other optional pieces. First attempt used only today's own logged weight (`entry?.weightKg`) and was reported live as still showing no reference first thing in the morning, before that day's own weigh-in. Second attempt switched to `useLatestWeight` (most recently logged weight across any past day) — also wrong, reported live with a screenshot: showed today's weight (59.6 kg) while the goal's week had actually started 2 days earlier at 58.8 kg, a materially different number, not just a timing gap. Fixed properly by reading `activeGoalProgress.baselineWeightKg` (`useActiveGoalProgress()`/`goalWindowProgress()`, already computed in this component for #235's target-met banner) — the weight logged on `goal.weekStart` itself, exactly the baseline #339's own "X → Y kg" past-target status line already reads from, not "whatever's most recently logged." New `t.today.weeklyTargetFromWeight()` dictionary key (both locales). 3 `TodayScreen.test.tsx` tests (absent with no weekStart weight logged, shown once it is, and — the regression case — still shows weekStart's weight rather than a later day's when the two differ). `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (91 tests) green. |
| [#470](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/470) | ✅ Done | Reorder button lives in the page header but only reorders the Stats accordion's cards | Confirmed on-device by the user. Reported live with screenshot: the "Reorder" button sits in the page-level header next to the "Today" title, making it look like it reorders the whole page, but it only drives the sortable stat-card group (`cardOrder`/`cardsByKey`) inside the Stats accordion further down (`TodayScreen.tsx` — button rendered in `PageHeader`'s `action` slot at the top, `DndContext`/`SortableContext` for the cards live inside the `Collapsible` below). Requested: move the button (and its Save/Reset companions) into the Stats accordion's own header. Removed the `action` prop from `PageHeader` entirely and moved the Reorder/Save/Reset controls into the Stats `Collapsible`'s own header row, next to its collapse/expand trigger. Also force-expands the section on entering reorder mode (previously independent of `statsCollapsed`) — the cards being reordered would otherwise stay hidden behind a collapsed trigger the reorder toggle now sits directly on. Live follow-up: swapped the chevron and Reorder button's positions — the chevron now trails at the very end of the row (conventional accordion placement) instead of sitting right after the "Stats" label with the Reorder button stranded further right. Split the single trigger `<button>` (label+chevron) into two sibling `CollapsibleTrigger`s — the label carries the real `aria-label`, the trailing chevron is a second, `aria-hidden`/`tabIndex={-1}` trigger — since a button can't nest inside another button, and the Reorder controls needed to sit between the two in the DOM. `tsc -b`/lint/`TodayScreen.test.tsx` (91 tests) green. |

## Tier 84 — Live feedback: meal card header wrap breaks layout (2026-08-01)

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#473](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/473) | ✅ Done | Meal card header wraps to a 2nd line and breaks layout (time/icons overlap) | Confirmed on-device by the user. Root cause: `{label} — {kcal} · {time}` was one `display:flex` header line, so a wrap left dangling time colliding with pencil/trash. Redesigned the meal card rather than only fixing wrap: **header** is meal name only (`min-w-0 flex-1`) with time pinned right beside the `shrink-0` icons; **totals line** is `macrosSummaryTextCompactWithCalories` (`2 430 ккал · Б 76г · Ж 176г · У 138г` / `P/F/C` in EN) at `text-base` — full-word macros wrapped badly in Russian; **dishes** use a muted StatCard hierarchy (name `text-base font-medium`, kcal `text-xl font-semibold tabular-nums`, macros `text-sm`, more `py-3`/`gap-3` spacing), dimmed so they don't compete with the meal title. New helper + i18n key; tests in `MealList`/`DailyEntryForm`/`TodayScreen`/`macroDisplay`. |
| [#474](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/474) | ✅ Done | Done button in add-meal flyout too small — primary footer actions should match Save button size | Confirmed on-device by the user. Root cause confirmed as filed: `AddMealDialog`'s Done was `<Button className="w-full">` — default `h-8` size with only a width override, so it rendered 16px shorter than every other footer CTA. Fixed both halves of the issue's scope: (1) Done now uses the footer-CTA size; (2) codified it as a **named `xl` size variant** in `shared/ui/button.tsx` (`h-12 … text-base`) rather than leaving the convention as a hand-written class string. `xl` deliberately reuses `lg`'s exact geometry (`gap-1.5 px-2.5`, only taller + `text-base`) so the 5 pre-existing `size="lg" className="h-12 w-full text-base"` call sites — `MealItemEditorSheet` Save + Save-and-add-another, `MealList` Copy-yesterday + Add-meal, `AddMealDialog` Repeat-meal — could all migrate to `size="xl" className="w-full"` with **zero** rendered-pixel change; they did, so the class string no longer exists anywhere to be copied inconsistently. Left alone deliberately: `RecipeEditorDialog`/`LogRecipeDialog`'s `size="lg"` footer buttons, which are right-aligned next to a ghost Cancel, not full-width CTAs — a different pattern, outside this issue's "full-width" framing. Regression test in `AddMealDialog.test.tsx` asserts Done is `h-12` and not `h-8`. `tsc -b`/lint/full suite green. |
| [#475](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/475) | ✅ Done | Edit dish sheet auto-focuses and selects the name field — easy to accidentally overwrite | Confirmed on-device by the user. Reported live with screenshot: opening Редактировать блюдо (`MealItemEditorSheet`) focused the name input with all text selected — one stray keystroke could delete/replace the dish name. Root cause: Radix Dialog FocusScope's default open auto-focus focuses the first tabbable and calls `.select()` on text inputs. Design fork resolved via `AskUserQuestion`: auto-focus only when *adding* a new dish, not when editing. New `autoFocusName?: boolean` prop on `MealItemEditorSheet` (default `true`); `AddMealDialog` passes `autoFocusName={!editingItemId}` so edit runs `onOpenAutoFocus` → `preventDefault()`. Regression test in `AddMealDialog.test.tsx`. `tsc -b`/lint/`AddMealDialog.test.tsx` green. |

Follow-up [#503](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/503): changing day via prev/next currently scrolls to the top — inconvenient when comparing a lower section (e.g. water) across days. |
| [#503](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/503) | ✅ Done | Day screen: keep scroll position when changing day via prev/next | Confirmed on-device by the user. Root cause: day switch set `entryStatus` to `'loading'`, which replaced the whole form with a short "Loading…" line and collapsed page height so the browser clamped scroll to the top. Fix: `setDate` captures `window.scrollY` and restores it after the new day is ready (`useLayoutEffect`); loading placeholder keeps the previous form's `minHeight` so the clamp doesn't fire mid-switch. Navigator-driven only — History `?date=` deep-links still land at the top. |
| [#500](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/500) | ✅ Done | MyFitnessPal import (#367): no password prompt; fails as not an MFP file | Confirmed on-device by the user. Confirmed against the user's real export: OLE/`D0CF` MS-OFFCRYPTO (AES-128-CBC + SHA1 agile), password in the same email. `isMyFitnessPalEncrypted` → Zepp-like password dialog; `officecrypto-tool` decrypts before exceljs. Plain unencrypted `.xlsx` still imports without a prompt. Wrong password stays in the dialog. `vite-plugin-node-polyfills` for crypto/buffer. **Failed first on-device validation**, then reproduced in desktop Chrome against the real file (headless Playwright + `vite preview`, since only a production build shows it): the decrypt chunk threw `ReferenceError: process is not defined`, surfacing as the same generic "not an MFP export" error. Cause was the CI follow-up that set `globals.process: false` — `theme.contrast.test.ts` reads `resolve(process.cwd(), …)` and the shim's `cwd()` returns '/'. Both needs are real, so the plugin is now skipped entirely when `mode === 'test'` (Vitest already runs in real Node) and fully enabled for dev/build. Verified end-to-end in Chrome: 2495 days imported. |
| [#501](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/501) | ✅ Done | Macro trend chart: chips, calories series, conditional y-axes, per-series chart type | Confirmed on-device by the user. All four asks built on existing precedents rather than new mechanics: `LineChart` → `ComposedChart`, static legend → a `ToggleGroup` chip row (#277's body-composition pattern), and `customChartPoints(entries, ['protein','fat','carbs','calories'])` reused wholesale for raw + per-series-normalized values, so no new domain math and no new i18n keys. Axis rule follows #330/#348/#393 exactly — 1 selected = one real axis, 2 = real left/right pair colored per series, 3+ = shared hidden normalized 0-100 with `customChartNormalizedCaveat` shown only in that case. Per-series line/bar/dots is #137's icon radiogroup lifted from `CustomChartView`; units live on the chips per #451. New `stores/macroChartSelectionStore.ts` (persisted under its own `turtle-steps-macro-chart-selection` key) holds both the selection and the per-series types — deliberately not shared with `customChartSelectionStore`, which would tie this chart's picker to "Compare your data"'s. **Two design forks resolved with the user before building**: calories is selected *by default* (all four on, so the default view is now the normalized 3+ mode rather than the old raw-grams axis), and the card title stays "Белки, жиры и углеводы" (it's also the drag/hide key label elsewhere). Side effect worth knowing: the chart's day set now includes days that logged calories but no macros, so the ≥3-day gate can pass on calories alone. |
| [#502](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/502) | ✅ Done | Compare Data: period marker looks like continuous multi-year menstruation | Confirmed on-device by the user. Reported live with screenshot: Weight + Менструация draws a thick continuous red band across years. **Three of the issue's own four suggested directions were already the shipped behavior** — boolean markers were dots-only, already `connectNulls={false}` with a `stroke="transparent"` line (no connecting stroke at all), and already have no line/bar/dots picker (only numeric series do). Real cause is dot *density*: each flagged day drew an 8px-wide dot (`r: 4`) while a day is a fraction of a pixel wide on a multi-year axis, so neighbouring dots overlapped into a solid band. Design fork resolved with the user (`AskQuestion`): keep dots and **group several days into one dot** on long ranges, rather than thin per-day ticks or hiding markers when zoomed out; placement stays at the top of the chart. New `booleanFlagMarkers(axisDates, flaggedDates, maxMarkers)` in `customChartSeries.ts` walks the x-axis in fixed-size windows and emits one dot per window that contains any flagged day, anchored on that window's *first* flagged day, then skips the rest of the window — so two dots can never land adjacent (a plain bucket-by-index grouping would let a run straddling a boundary emit two touching dots). `MAX_BOOLEAN_MARKERS = 24` assumes the ~330px phone plot area and 8px dots, leaving visible background between neighbours; when the axis is short enough the window size is 1 and every flagged day keeps its own dot, i.e. pre-#502 behavior exactly. Two supporting additions: boolean flags now get a **tooltip row** at all (they never had one), showing `N days` when a dot is grouped, and a caveat line under the chart rendered *only* while grouping is actually in effect. `dayCount()`/`ruPluralize` reused for the Russian count. |
| [#505](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/505) | ✅ Done | Add meal / Add food modals: match Day meal-card typography and spacing (#473) | Confirmed on-device by the user. Shared #473 type/spacing tokens (not tan card fill): dialog titles `text-lg font-medium`; composition/Recent shells `rounded-xl`/`p-4`/`divide`/`py-3`; dish name `text-base font-medium`, composition + quantity/editor kcal `text-xl font-semibold tabular-nums`, day totals `text-base`, macros/secondary `text-sm`; one `gap-3`/`gap-4` body scale; quieter quick-action tiles; quantity inputs `h-12`. Optional shared dish-row primitive deferred. |
| [#506](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/506) | ✅ Done | Overview averages: show from→to date range on 7-day / 30-day cards | Confirmed on-device by the user. Reported live with screenshot. Cards keep «Последние 7/30 дней» and now show the inclusive from→to calendar range on the description line (same `weekRange` + `'PP'` formatting as weekly summary). New `recentAverageWindowRange()` mirrors `recentAverages`' today-anchored window (`today` inclusive, `windowDays - 1` days back). Empty days stay in the shown range but are still skipped in the average denominator. |
| [#507](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/507) | ✅ Done | Add meal: eye toggle to completely hide Recent suggestions | Confirmed on-device by the user. Live ask with screenshot. Reused #245/#232 eye pattern via `SectionTitleWithToggle` + new persisted `useAddMealRecentVisibilityStore` (`turtle-steps-add-meal-recent-visibility`). Hides the list; title + eye stay to restore; Show all only while visible. Personal library untouched. |
| [#508](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/508) | ✅ Done | Add meal modal: regroup header CTAs (time / clear / delete / close) | Confirmed on-device by the user. Reported live with screenshot. Close alone top-right; clear inside time field; delete meal to ⋯ or sticky-footer secondary — not four CTAs in one corner. Delete placement fork resolved with the user (`AskQuestion`): **footer secondary**, not a `⋯` menu — a one-item overflow menu isn't worth a new primitive, and this keeps destructive furthest from dismiss. Header now holds title + one time widget: the #117 clear ✕ sits *inside* a shared bordered wrapper with the `type="time"` input (wrapper owns the border/`focus-within` ring, input goes `border-transparent`), so it no longer reads as a second bare ✕ beside DialogContent's Close. Whole-meal delete (#459) became a quiet labelled `Delete meal` ghost button in destructive colour, with #459's two-step confirm rendered in the same slot. New `deleteWholeMealButton` i18n key for the visible text; positioned `deleteMealLabel(n)` stays as its aria-label so the Day-row and dialog deletes stay distinguishable (and existing tests keep matching). Rendered in two mutually exclusive spots, same shape as #480's `mealNoteField`: inside the reaction/note block for a meal with items, and in the browse body when a saved meal has none (no items → no "this meal so far" block → no sticky footer at all). **Found only in live Playwright checking**: placing it *between* that block and the sticky footer made it invisible whenever content was short enough not to scroll — the sticky Done bar covers that last strip, which is exactly what the block's own `pb-20` clearance exists for, so the button had to go inside it. |

Edit buffers in `editingMealDraft` until Done; Close on dirty asks discard (new copy). Composition trash confirms before remove (new + edit). Empty draft + Done deletes the meal. |

History's combined `DailyEntryForm` bumped `gap-4`→`gap-6` so shells match. |

Final: same muted shell and pencil, values in a `grid-cols-3` label/value grid (two rows, five cells) at identical weight, fixed height dropped so the two rows are the designed layout. ## Tier 86 — Retroactive: CI-red flaky test filed after the fact (2026-08-01)

_Reported by the user as "the build is failing" on the #511 run; same category as Tier 57/59/80's own retroactive flake fixes._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#512](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/512) | ✅ Done | CI: AddMealDialog barcode test flaky — waits on a dish name the confirm step unmounts | Run [30710491495](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/actions/runs/30710491495) (the #511 commit) failed on `AddMealDialog.test.tsx`'s barcode test alone, 1610/1611 passing; the next run (#509) passed with the test untouched, and #511 changed nothing in this file. **Not** a timeout, despite this test's own bump history (1000 → 10000 → 20000ms): it failed ~233ms in, and the error was `toBeInTheDocument()` on an *already-resolved* element — `findByText('Protein Bar')` resolved against a node that was detached again before the assertion. Cause: `AddMealDialog` renders `activeItem ? <quantity-confirm step> : <search + Recent list>` as exclusive branches, and the test seeds `Protein Bar` into the personal library (`mealItemStore.touch(...)`, required so `lookupBarcode` resolves it locally), so the same name can render as a **Recent** row while the scan chain (dynamic `@zxing` import → mocked decode callback → `lookupBarcode`'s IndexedDB read) is still in flight. Under full-suite CI load store hydration wins that race, `findByText` grabs the Recent row, then `setActiveItem` swaps branches and unmounts it. Fixed by awaiting the confirm step itself — `+ Add item` exists only in that branch, so it can't resolve early — and asserting the dish name after it mounts; the 20s timeout stays, the async chain genuinely needs it. No release note (#131/#144/#349/#400 precedent — test-only change). |

## Tier 89 — Live feedback: correlation calories vs after-midnight meals (2026-08-02)

_Reported live with correlation + Day screenshots and `turtle-steps-backup-2026-08-02.json`: Overview «Калории и изменение веса» tooltip shows 727 kcal for 1 Aug 2026, but that day's logged meals sum to 1244 kcal including a 01:09 post-midnight meal (328 kcal) that appears on the Day screen._

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |
|---|--------|-------|-------|
| [#522](https://github.com/ZhannaM85/turtle-steps-to-the-goal/issues/522) | ✅ Done | Dashboard correlation calories wrong for days with after-midnight meals (shows 727 vs ~1244 on 1 Aug) | Confirmed on-device by the user. Not an after-midnight bug — 01:09 meal was already in the 1244 day total. Tooltip showed weekly *average* for the incomplete week of 1–2 Aug (Sat week-start from period filter): (1244+210)/2 = 727. Fix: `correlationInsightPoints` excludes incomplete/current weeks (`weekEnd` after `min(today, max entry date)`, plus leading weeks truncated by the period start). Sparse finished historical weeks still count |

| # | Status | Issue | Notes |

Root cause: `DashboardScreen` subscribed to whole `byChart`, so one toggle re-rendered every section. Fix: per-chart `useDashboardChartPeriod` / `usePeriodFilteredEntries`; screen no longer reads the period store.
